    OC        b>1, r<=t; a=0 or a=1 or r>|a-1|t             r/t+a

    CO        [b<=1 or r>b-1]; a>1, r+b<=a-1               (r+b)/(a-1)

    OO        [b<=1 or r>b-1];
              [a<=1 or r+b>a-1]                            r+a+b.     (7.1)

In CC, the condition a!=0,1 is read together with a>=0, so a>0. In OC,
the last disjunct is only needed for a not equal to0 or1. Boundaries are
assigned to core except for the identity convention. A row may be empty;
their union is never empty. In particular the OO row contains all sufficiently
large r. At a=0,b=0,r=1, the identity endpoint is assigned to OO and has
value1. No formula that divides by a-1 is applied to that identity step.

The formulas display every division and its strictly positive denominator.
They also show that the only branch breakpoints on r>=C can be drawn from

    b-1, |a-1|(b-1), a-b-1.                         (7.2)

Not every listed number is an active breakpoint, which causes no problem.
On each nonempty interval of the partition, write Dcap_ab(r)=A r+B.
Here A>0 and B>=0. Direct differentiation, within that interval only, gives

    d/dr [(2r-d)/(A r+B)^2]
      =2[B+A(d-r)]/(A r+B)^3.                       (7.3)

Thus its sole possible stationary maximum is r=d+B/A. For the four rows
the possible stationary radii are respectively

    d,  d+a(b-1),  d+b,  d+a+b.                     (7.4)

Consequently an EXACT finite formula for the optimum over every allowed r is

    max_{r in Ecap_ab,S} (2r-d)/[mcap_a(mcap_b(r))]^2,               (7.5)

where Ecap_ab,S is the following explicit list after deleting entries less
than C and deleting repetitions:

    C, b-1, |a-1|(b-1), a-b-1,
    d, d+a(b-1), d+b, d+a+b.                         (7.6)

These are at most eight analytic candidate expressions; this draft does not
numerically enumerate or evaluate a parameter grid. Extra inactive candidates
are allowed because they are still legal radii in the original domain.

For completeness, an excluded right-hand interval endpoint cannot conceal a
larger supremum. On positive arguments mcap_h is left-continuous, with only
upward jumps to its right. Its composition has the same property: if an inner
map jumps across an outer breakpoint, the outer monotonicity preserves the
upward direction. Therefore Vcap has only downward jumps to its right. The
value at a breakpoint is at least the limiting value of any immediately
following interval. Formula(7.3) handles the interiors, C is included, and
Vcap(r) tends to0 at infinity since the final row is OO. Every possible
maximum is therefore attained at a listed breakpoint or stationary point.
The nonempty list contains C. This proves(7.5) even at every zero/unit-step
degeneracy.

## 8. The paid continuation invariant exists at arbitrary depth

For any finite nonnegative prefix word w=(w_0,...,w_(p-1)), define the explicit
map

    Mcap_w(t)=mcap_(w_0)(mcap_(w_1)(...mcap_(w_(p-1))(t)...)),
    Mcap_empty(t)=t.                                 (8.1)

This is composition of the CLOSED formulas(4.2), not an unspecified feasible
set, optimization oracle, or Bellman lemma. It is the exact smallest modulus
of an initial point whose GD run on Hcap under the original w ends at a given
signed target of modulus t. Induction proves this: for the first step,
minimize over possible later starting moduli; monotonicity of mcap_(w_0)
shows that the smallest available later modulus is optimal, and the signed
choice in Section4 attains it. Equivalently one can induct by prepending a
step and apply the two inequalities in Section5.

For words u,v, where u followed by v denotes their original chronological
concatenation,

    Mcap_(u followed by v)=Mcap_u composed with Mcap_v.              (8.2)

All these maps are nondecreasing and strictly increasing for positive input.
Their exact state on(0,infinity) can be kept as at most p+1 intervals with
affine formulas A t+B, A>0,B>=0, and the values at their breakpoints. This
assertion is symbolic, not a computation: start with the single piece t.
Prepending one mcap_h leaves all existing breakpoints and introduces at most
one more, because a strictly increasing inner map can cross its sole positive
threshold |1-h| at most once. If the crossing occurs within an affine piece,
the new threshold is explicitly (|1-h|-B)/A. If a jump crosses it, it uses an
already existing breakpoint. h=0 adds nothing and h=1 is t+1 on positive
inputs, also adding nothing. The composition coefficients remain A>0,B>=0.

For the old suffix jets(3.1), a fixed r>=C, and any such prefix, the actual
function, minimum and suffix remain(2.1),(3.3). The true paid quantities are

    initial distance = eta Mcap_w(r),
    terminal gap = L eta^2(r-S-1/2),
    normalized payoff = (2r-2S-1)/Mcap_w(r)^2.         (8.3)

All gradients and values are actual samples of the SAME static function,
and Section6 still checks every remote row. This proves scalable feasibility
and exact normalization in this restricted family for arbitrary h, including
all zero and unit entries. It does not prove the original-B lower comparison.

For perspective, if every entry of w is strictly less than2 and the incoming
target is r>=1, every inverse branch is outer: its input remains at least1,
while its core threshold is less than1. Thus Mcap_w(r)=r+sum w. Optimizing
the positive-affine final-state family then gives just the corresponding
global sum-based Huber value. Equality h=2 is genuinely different at target1
and must not be silently put into this observation.

## 9. Complete SAME-word comparison for two preceding steps

We now compare EVERY construction of Sections3--7 with accepted witnesses
on exactly the original word(5.1). No tail entry is reordered or replaced.

The following three entries belong to K_(m+2)(h) by b98bb807d514e80d:

    K_global=1/[d+2a+2b],
    K_first=(a-1)_+^2/[d+2b],
    K_second=(b-1)_+^2/[(1+a)^2 d].                 (9.1)

They need not be the COMPLETE K, since there are also possible cuts inside
the suffix. Bounding by any one entry is enough to bound by the complete K.

If a>1, b>1, put P=(a-1)(b-1). For the checkpoint choice a,b in accepted
1fca3e5dd555cb4a, the ordinary blocks before a and between a,b are empty,
and the final ordinary block is the unchanged k with sum S. Its recurrence
therefore gives

    m_0=1, m_1=a-1, O_0=O_1=0,
    z_term=P, m_old=1, e_term=0.

Its precise feasibility requirement is P>=1+S=C. In that domain the allowed
last slope is q in [1,P/C], and the optimized value is

    R_two(P)=P^2/d                  if P>=d,
               2P-d                if C<=P<=d.       (9.2)

Indeed q_opt=max(1,P/d). If P>=d then P/d<=P/C since d>=C, so it is
feasible and gives P^2/d. If P<=d the feasible maximum is q=1 and gives
2P-d. At P=d the formulas agree. Thus (9.2) is a legal entry of the
COMPLETE F_ray_(m+2)(h), with every remote ray offset and value condition
retained. We only invoke it after proving P>=C in the relevant branch.

Here are the four comparisons, using the actual table(7.1).

OO. Here Dcap=r+a+b. For any r>0 and c>=0,

    (r+c)^2-(d+2c)(2r-d)=(r-d-c)^2>=0.

Take c=a+b to get Vcap<=1/(d+2a+2b)=K_global.

CO. Here a>1 and Dcap=(r+b)/(a-1). Use the same square with c=b:

    Vcap=(a-1)^2(2r-d)/(r+b)^2
         <=(a-1)^2/(d+2b)=K_first.

OC. Here t=b-1>0, r<=t, and Dcap=r/t+a. Put u=r/t in(0,1].
The identity (tu-d)^2>=0 gives

    2tu-d <= t^2 u^2/d.

Because a>=0 and u<=1,

    u/(u+a) <= 1/(1+a),

as follows by multiplying the positive denominators and cancelling u.
Therefore

    Vcap=(2tu-d)/(u+a)^2
         <=t^2/d [u/(u+a)]^2
         <=t^2/[(1+a)^2 d]=K_second.

CC with 0<a<1. Put P=(1-a)t. Then Dcap=r/P and

    Vcap=P^2(2r-d)/r^2 <=P^2/d,

using (r-d)^2>=0. Also (1-a)(1+a)=1-a^2<=1, so

    P^2/d <= t^2/[(1+a)^2d]=K_second.

CC with a>1. Put P=(a-1)t. The exact branch condition gives r<=P,
and r>=C proves P>=C. Thus the ray witness(9.2) IS feasible. Write
phi_d(r)=(2r-d)/r^2. The square(r-d)^2>=0 gives phi_d(r)<=1/d.
If P>=d this proves Vcap=P^2 phi_d(r)<=P^2/d=R_two(P).
If C<=P<=d, the derivative

    phi_d'(r)=2(d-r)/r^3

is nonnegative for r<=P<=d. Hence

    Vcap=P^2 phi_d(r)<=P^2 phi_d(P)=2P-d=R_two(P).

This exhausts the domain, including every equality case by the branch
conventions. No bound on a,b or S has been imposed. Consequently

    for all parameters(5.4) and EVERY two-step inverse branch,
    actual normalized payoff <= F_ray_(m+2)(h).      (9.3)

Taking the exact maximum(7.5) preserves(9.3). The conclusion covers every
minimum position/value obtained by varying r, eta, X,V in this family.
It is therefore a complete obstruction to gaining a NEW lower witness from
this two-preceding-step capped-quadratic extension alone. It is not merely
a comparison with K_global or with a selected weak Huber channel.

For avoidance of a quantifier error: the above is an UPPER bound on a
specified witness FAMILY. It is not an upper bound on W_(m+2)(h), whose
adversary class is much larger. A bad word or a failed witness family is
not a minimax disproof.

## 10. Exact nontrivial example and the anchor discontinuity

Take a=b=3 and suffix k=(1), so S=1,C=2,d=3. At r=2 use eta=L=1,
xi=mu=0, equivalently X=2,V=3/2. The exact minimal predecessor trajectory is

    z_0=1/2, z_1=-1, z_2=2, z_3=1,
    gradients=(1/2,-1,1,1),
    values=(1/8,1/2,3/2,1/2).

Each update is the ORIGINAL sequence(3,3,1):

    1/2-3(1/2)=-1,
    -1-3(-1)=2,
    2-1(1)=1.

The unique minimum is0 at0. The true initial squared distance is1/4 and
the true terminal gap is1/2, so W-normalized payoff is4.

This is the OPTIMUM of the entire displayed suffix-preserving family for
this word: its domain is r>=2. At r=2 both inverse branches are core and
give4. At every r>2 both are outer, so

    Vcap(r)=(2r-3)/(r+6)^2<=1/15,

by the OO comparison, with equality at r=9. Thus the overall maximum is4.

The COMPLETE scalar K for this same word is

    max{1/15, 4/9, 1/12, 0}=4/9.

The legal two-cut ray uses m_0=1,m_1=2,P=4,q=4/3 and has payoff16/3.
This is also the complete F_ray here. A checkpoint set including the last
step1 and at least one earlier checkpoint is infeasible: if m_last>0 denotes
the slope just before the last checkpoint and O>=0 the sum of its ray offsets,
its final z_term=(1-1)m_last-O<=0 while the previous terminal-ray slope is
strictly positive. Selecting step1 alone gives0. The only remaining
possibilities are the empty set, the two single3 cuts, and both3 cuts,
whose values are exactly those just listed. Therefore

    K_3(3,3,1)=4/9 < 4 < F_ray_3(3,3,1)=16/3.       (10.1)

The nine-fold gain over K does not beat the already accepted stronger
witness on the SAME word. No schedule-improvement claim follows from it.

Now keep the ORIGINAL suffix jets X=2,V=3/2,eta=L=1 fixed and change
the relative anchor to r=2+epsilon for any epsilon>0. The function(2.1)
has true minimizer xi=-epsilon and true minimum mu=-epsilon, while its old
suffix positions2,1, gradients1,1 and values3/2,1/2 remain unchanged.
The b=3 core preimage disappears because r>2. Therefore

    Dcap_3,3(2)=1/2,
    Dcap_3,3(2+epsilon)=8+epsilon,
    Vcap(2+epsilon)=(1+2epsilon)/(8+epsilon)^2.       (10.2)

For epsilon>0 the actual minimizing path has dimensionless points
8+epsilon,5+epsilon,2+epsilon,1+epsilon, and therefore actual points
8,5,2,1. Its gradients are all1 and its actual values are
15/2,9/2,3/2,1/2. These match the moved minimum and the old suffix.
As epsilon decreases to0 the true-distance payoff tends to1/64, whereas
its value AT epsilon=0 is4. The discontinuity is in the minimum-radius
inverse selection, not in the validity of any of the individual functions.
Both the numerator and the distance in(10.2) include the moving minimum.

This explicitly refutes continuity, and any unqualified smooth parameter
update assumption, for this minimum-radius backwards state. It does not
claim discontinuity of the unrestricted interpolation problem or of W.

## 11. All-depth obstruction: exact soft-threshold schedules

This section strengthens the scope of the closure from two preceding steps
to the entire scalar family(2.1), with ANY terminal regime and any initial
point. The earlier unverified binary counterexample e6841b2627f03bfd prompted
a memory check; nothing from it is assumed as a fact. The full proof follows.

For A>=0 write

    Rsh_A(z)=sign(z) max(|z|-A,0),

with Rsh_A(0)=0. This notation is a soft-threshold map, not the project's
objective normalization R_n. If A>=1 and h>=0, then the exact identity

    Rsh_A composed with Tcap_h = Rsh_(A+h)           (11.1)

holds IF AND ONLY IF h<=A+1.

Proof of sufficiency: both sides are odd, so take z>=0. For 0<=z<=1,
Tcap_h(z)=(1-h)z has absolute value at most |1-h|<=A, since h<=A+1
and A>=1. The left side vanishes, as does the right side because A+h>=1.
For z>=1, Tcap_h(z)=z-h. If z-h<0, its negative modulus is h-z<=h-1<=A,
so the left side vanishes, again agreeing with the right side. If z-h>=0,
the left side is max(z-h-A,0), exactly the right side. This proves(11.1)
on every real z. For necessity, if h>A+1, take z=1. The left side equals
-(h-1-A)<0, whereas the right side is0. Thus the identity fails. The zero
step is included in the sufficient condition and gives the identity map.

Now let a nonempty original word w end with a physical unit step, and suppose
each preceding step satisfies the explicit tail-sum condition

    w_i <= 1+sum_{j>i} w_j.                         (11.2)

Let H=sum_i w_i. The last unit step equals Rsh_1. Applying(11.1) successively
backwards proves that the COMPLETE forward GD map under w is Rsh_H. Every
tail used in the induction has sum at least1, so every hypothesis of(11.1)
is met. Zero steps at any preceding positions are allowed. Trailing zero
steps may also be appended without changing the map. No order restriction
beyond(11.2) is needed for this claim.

We next optimize over the ENTIRE family(2.1), including all possible terminal
core points, while paying the true minimum and distance. By translation,
scaling and oddness, let the initial dimensionless radius be z>0. If z<=H,
the terminal point is the minimizer and the payoff is0. If z>H, write the
terminal radius as v=z-H>0. The normalized payoff is

    2 Hcap(v)/(H+v)^2.

For 0<v<=1 this equals v^2/(H+v)^2, an increasing function of v because
H>=1. Its maximum on that interval is1/(H+1)^2. For v>=1 it equals
(2v-1)/(H+v)^2. Differentiation gives

    2(H+1-v)/(H+v)^3,

so its maximum occurs at v=H+1 and equals1/(2H+1). The inequality
(H+1)^2>=2H+1 shows that the latter is the overall maximum. Therefore

    sup over ALL eta,xi,mu,x_0!=xi in family(2.1)
        2(f(x_n)-mu)/(L|x_0-xi|^2) = 1/(1+2H).     (11.3)

The value is attained at dimensionless initial radius2H+1, ending at H+1.
In particular changing the whole-word-dependent scale, moving the minimum,
or using a terminal core instead of the affine suffix cannot repair(11.3).

For a polynomial-size example choose the actual length-n word

    w_dec^(n)=(n,n-1,...,2,1), n>=1,
    H=n(n+1)/2.

A step j>=2 has tail sum j(j-1)/2, and

    j <= 1+j(j-1)/2

is equivalent to (j-1)(j-2)>=0. Thus(11.2) holds. Formula(11.3) gives the
EXACT entire-family value

    Vcap_dec(n)=1/[1+n(n+1)].                       (11.4)

Accepted b98bb807d514e80d supplies B_n<=1+4n^p, p=log_2(3)<2. Its
applicability is only the original benchmark recurrence and the present
length n; no schedule restriction is involved. Hence

    0 <= Vcap_dec(n)/U_n^ZJ
      = B_n/[1+n(n+1)]
      <= (1+4n^p)/[1+n(n+1)] -> 0.                 (11.5)

On EXACTLY the same word, the first-step scalar cut from the same accepted
fact gives, for n>=2,

    W_n(w_dec^(n)) >= K_n(w_dec^(n))
                   >= (n-1)^2/[1+n(n-1)] -> 1.      (11.6)

Therefore the exhibited small restricted-family value says nothing small
about the unrestricted W_n; indeed a previously accepted scalar witness is
already much stronger. Since F_ray>=K, the full accepted ray reference also
dominates this calibration. Equations(11.5)--(11.6) are a scoped obstruction
to the family, not a negative answer to the original minimax question.

For comparison with the RECALLED old finding, descending binary steps
(2^(n-1),2^(n-2),...,1) have H=2^n-1 and satisfy equality in(11.2) at every
preceding position. Formula(11.3), proved here independently, specializes
to1/(2^(n+1)-1), exactly the earlier recorded family obstruction. This is
a consistency check, not a new claim of discovering that obstruction.

## 12. What has and has not been closed

Closed in this UNVERIFIED joint draft:

1. A concrete positive-payoff suffix with independent old location/value,
   a free scale, and an explicit range of moving minimum anchors.
2. The ACTUAL two-preceding-step extension on the original word, including
   ALL inverse branches, joint earlier points/values, h=0,h=1 and arbitrarily
   large steps, the full nonempty domain, every ordered row, and true radius.
3. Exact optimization of its remaining relative anchor via at most eight
   analytic candidates, with jumps and boundary ownership accounted for.
4. An explicit paid continuation invariant for this SAME static-function
   family at arbitrary depth, without a hidden feasibility oracle.
5. A complete SAME-word domination theorem for its ENTIRE two-step extension
   family by accepted scalar/alternating-ray references, and a sharp finite
   example showing why comparison only to K is inadequate.
6. An exact all-depth soft-threshold composition criterion, with a complete
   whole-family payoff optimization. It re-establishes the already known
   scoped failure of full-curvature symmetric Hubers and gives a descending
   linear word whose restricted ratio to the ORIGINAL B profile tends to0.

Still OPEN:

* A useful original-B estimate for a genuinely larger continuation family.
  The maps(8.1) are exact feasibility/payoff invariants for their fixed family,
  but Section11 proves that they CANNOT satisfy the required all-word original-B
  lower estimate. That family-level question is closed, not merely unproved.
* Whether a richer suffix family, asymmetric clipping, additional affine
  offsets, or genuine transverse geometry can escape the two-step domination
  while retaining a paid all-row continuation rule. Merely allowing an interior
  terminal jet in the unchanged symmetric family does not escape Section11.
* The original arbitrary-h exact-profile minimax problem in PROBLEM.md.

One must not apply(9.3) repeatedly without a new proof. After a general earlier
inverse step the retained portion contains core and/or alternating gradients,
and is not the positive constant-gradient suffix assumed in Section3. Resetting
the clipping threshold or anchor to restore that assumption would in general
change old jets. Likewise the accepted ConPD washout theorem has schedule
hypotheses not furnished by(8.2); scalar map composition is not an all-function
certificate composition.

Suggested remaining analytical round: retain the paid minimum/jet bookkeeping
but select a genuinely larger concrete suffix family, such as asymmetric
clipping or a jointly constructed nonhomogeneous transverse support. Do not
retune the closed symmetric full-curvature family, its threshold law, or its
moving-minimum parametrization in hope of recovering the target. A lower core
curvature is outside this obstruction but needs a paid whole-word rule, not
a free parameter claim. Any new lemma must be reproved in a new joint draft
or separately accepted before it can be used as a fact.

No fact_submit was authorized or called. All claims above remain UNVERIFIED
until a future selected gate accepts the final object. No external citation is
used as a new black-box premise, and no CPU computation supplies a proof step.

      >= C*(s_i+1)+(c*(c-2)-C)*(1+S_j).

This lower bound, coupled to the unchanged first-radius formula(43),

# Variable-matrix terminal-credit repair along saturated right tails

Status: complete self-contained candidate, NOT verifier-accepted. No numerical
solver and no fact_submit call. This is a certificate theorem in a specified
unbounded-length class, not the unrestricted primitive-cone theorem and not an
objective-profile bound. The only accepted background used is the actual
interpolation inequality from fact 1252b8823bb5a290. All additional algebra and
the short-leaf repair are proved here rather than imported from findings.

## 1. The exact certificate class and result

For a positive list h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), write s=sum h_i, C=1+s,
x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t, and

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                                   -sC||g_n||^2,
 J_h=sum_(i<n)h_i Q_(i,n),   E_theta(h)=V_h-theta J_h.

All n+1 formal gradients are independent. A signed terminal certificate is
an array lambda_ij on distinct finite pairs such that:

 (a) V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij cancels EVERY formal f_i coefficient;
 (b) the resulting FULL quadratic form M_h is positive semidefinite;
 (c) lambda_ij>=0 whenever j!=n, while lambda_(i,n) may be any real number.

An ordinary certificate additionally has lambda_(i,n)>=0. A nonnegative
certificate for E_theta is exactly a signed certificate with
lambda_(i,n)>=-theta h_i: to pass from the signed to the actual E_theta
array, add theta h_i on i->n. No interpolation inequality is replaced.

Define a class R of positive schedules recursively. Every list of length at
most two belongs to R. A longer list belongs to R if it has a decomposition

 h=(a,1+r,b),   A=1+sum a,   D=1+sum b,
 T=1+sum h=A+D+r,   r>0,   rT=AD,                 (1)

where a is ANY positive list (possibly empty) and b belongs to R. No
primitivity assumption is imposed on a. Fix one such finite decomposition
chain when a schedule has several choices. The word "right" only indicates
that the recursion continues inside b. The earlier lists a can be arbitrarily
long and need not themselves have a decomposition.

Candidate theorem. Every signed terminal certificate for any h in R can be
converted explicitly into an ordinary certificate for that SAME h. The
construction is allowed to change the full PSD remainder. More precisely,
at each cut (1), a supplied certificate forces an ordinary certificate for a
and a signed certificate for b. If the input has the E_theta lower bounds,
0<=theta<1, then the inherited tail bounds are exactly those of E_theta_b,
where

 kappa=(T/D)^2=(A/r)^2,
 theta_b=1-(1-theta)/kappa.                        (2)

The construction uses every actual multiplier, value flow, and full matrix.
It remains valid for nonnegative schedules whose positive compression is in
R, with a final certificate on all original independent gradients.

The assumptions include existence of the stated finite certificate. No dual
attainment theorem identifying arbitrary universal validity with this
certificate class is asserted.

## 2. A saturated cut has two explicit equality trajectories

Fix (1). Let L be the bridge query, so a occupies steps 0,...,L-1.
Call queries 0,...,L the prefix group P. The tail group B comprises the query
immediately after the bridge, all subsequent tail queries, and final query z.
Use local tail indices j=0,...,m, with z=m, and always embed them into the
original list when they occur in Q. Put p=g_L, q=g_z,
H_b=sum_(j<m)b_j g_j, and eta=r/D=A/T.

First use a scalar unit-threshold Huber function, starting sufficiently far
out on its positive affine ray that every query has gradient 1. It is convex,
differentiable, and 1-smooth. Every finite Q is zero and

 V_h=2sum_i h_i sum_(t=i)^(n-1)h_t
          +sum_i h_i(1-h_i)-s(s+1)=0.

Here 2sum_i h_i sum_(t=i)^(n-1)h_t=s^2+sum_i h_i^2.

The second scalar function is obtained by integrating

 f'(x)=min(1,max(x,-eta)),   f(0)=0,

and starts at x_0=A. Its derivative is continuous, nondecreasing, and
1-Lipschitz, so it is a legal convex 1-smooth function with minimizer 0.
If R_i^a is the remaining prefix step sum at query i in P, its prefix data are

 x_i=1+R_i^a,   g_i=1,   f_i=R_i^a+1/2.

At L, R_L^a=0. The bridge lands at -r. At a tail query with remaining tail
sum R_j^b, the data are

 x_j=-eta(1+R_j^b),   g_j=-eta,
 f_j=eta^2(R_j^b+1/2).

In particular x_z=g_z=-eta. All same-group Q are zero. Expanding the displayed
data gives every cross residual:

 Q_(i,j)=-(1+eta)eta R_j^b,   i in P, j in B,
 Q_(j,i)=-(1+eta)R_i^a,       i in P, j in B.        (3)

For example the first expression is
R_i^a+1/2-eta^2(R_j^b+1/2)-eta(1+R_j^b)
 -(1+R_i^a)+(1+eta)^2/2. The second expression follows by swapping the
roles in the definition of Q and equals -(1+eta)R_i^a.

Every incoming-terminal residual is zero: j=z has R_z^b=0 and same-group
tail residuals are zero. At every query one also has
f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2. Telescoping squared positions therefore gives

 2sum_(i<n)h_i f_i+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
     =||x_0||^2-||x_z||^2,

and consequently V_h=A^2-T^2 eta^2=0 by eta=A/T.

## 3. Complementarity forces the full mixed block, even with signed credit

Let lambda be any supplied signed certificate from Section 1. On either
equality trajectory, V_h=0 and every term having a potentially negative
coefficient has Q_(i,z)=0. All other coefficients are nonnegative and their
actual Q are nonpositive, by (3) or fact 1252b8823bb5a290. Hence

 0<=M_h=V_h+sum lambda Q<=0.

Every term with a strictly negative Q must therefore have coefficient zero.
Because the steps inside each list are positive, (3) leaves only these cross
edges:

 nu_i=lambda_(i,z), i in P;     mu_j=lambda_(j,L), j in B.     (4)

The nu_i are initially allowed either sign; all mu_j are nonnegative.
The two equality gradient vectors are e_P+e_B and e_P-eta e_B. A PSD matrix
annihilates a vector on which its quadratic form is zero (diagonalize the
real symmetric matrix, whose eigenvalues are nonnegative). Since eta>0,
both block indicators e_P and e_B lie in the kernel of the FULL M_h.

The only mixed prefix-tail terms remaining after (4) are

 -sum_(i in P)nu_i <g_i,H_b+q>
       +r sum_(j in B)mu_j <p,g_j>.                (5)

To check (5), the prefix-to-terminal position term has tail contribution
-<g_i,H_b>, and its squared difference contributes -<g_i,q>.
For j->L, the position contribution involving p is (1+r)<g_j,p>, while the
squared difference contributes -<g_j,p>, leaving r<g_j,p>.
All within-group Q and V_h have no mixed term.

For i<L, row i of M_h e_B is -D nu_i/2. Thus
nu_i=0 for every i<L. Rows j in B of M_h e_P then give

 mu_j=(nu_L/r)b_j for j<m,    mu_z=nu_L/r.          (6)

Value cancellation in V_h+sum lambda Q means that outgoing minus incoming
flow at every nonterminal index i is -2h_i. Summing through P yields
sum_j mu_j-nu_L=2(A+r). Using (6) gives

 nu_L=2r(A+r)/(D-r)=r(kappa-1),
 mu_j=(kappa-1)b_j (j<m),     mu_z=kappa-1.         (7)

Here D>r, kappa>1, and
(kappa-1)(D-r)=2(A+r), obtained by substituting rT=AD and T=A+D+r.
All mixed coefficients in (5) now vanish individually. Thus the full
prefix-tail matrix is zero, not just its value on the equality trajectories.

This proof permits arbitrary negative incoming-terminal coefficients. Their
signs never enter the complementarity step because the corresponding Q all
vanish on both equality runs.

## 4. Full energy identity and the exact inherited credit

Let

 J_b=sum_(j<m)b_j Q_(j,z),
 C_cross=sum_(j<m)b_j Q_(j,L)+Q_(z,L)+r Q_(L,z).

The identity needed for restriction and rebuilding is

 V_h=V_a+kappa V_b-(kappa-1)(C_cross+J_b).          (8)

Here V_a has terminal query L, and V_b uses the original tail queries.
For a full coefficient check put C_aux=C_cross+J_b. Its value and gradient
parts respectively are

 C_aux,f=2sum_(j<m)b_j f_j+(r-D)f_L+(2-D-r)f_z,
 C_aux,g=sum_(j<m)b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2
          +[(D-r)/2-r^2]||p||^2+(D+r)||q||^2/2.    (9)

The identity
2sum_j b_j<g_j,sum_(i<j)b_i g_i>
 =||H_b||^2-sum_j b_j^2||g_j||^2
gives (9) by expanding the four kinds of Q in C_aux; the mixed products
in p,H_b,q cancel. Inserting (9) into (8) matches the prefix coefficients
unchanged. It matches the bridge value and squared-gradient coefficients
2(1+r) and -r(1+r), and terminal coefficients -2(T-1) and -T(T-1).
These matches use

 (kappa-1)(D-r)=2(A+r),  kappa r^2=A^2,  kappa D^2=T^2.

Let lambda^a, lambda^b be the supplied within-group arrays. Define

 lambda_hat^b_ij=lambda^b_ij/kappa
                   -(1-1/kappa)b_i 1_(i<m,j=z).    (10)

Equations (7)-(8) give the exact formal identity

 M_h=M_a+kappa M_b,
 M_a=V_a+sum lambda^a_ij Q_ij,
 M_b=V_b+sum lambda_hat^b_ij Q_ij.                 (11)

The prefix and tail values are disjoint, so cancellation of all original
values implies separate cancellation in (11). Their formal gradients are
disjoint, and the mixed block is zero, so PSD of the full M_h implies PSD of
BOTH full blocks. The prefix coefficients are nonnegative because their
targets differ from z. The tail coefficients are nonnegative except possibly
those into z. Thus (11) is an ordinary certificate for a and a signed one
for b, as claimed.

If lambda_(i,z)>=-theta h_i, then (10) gives

 lambda_hat^b_(i,z)>=-[1-(1-theta)/kappa]b_i.

Every other tail sign remains nonnegative. This proves (2), with
0<=theta_b<1 when 0<=theta<1. No lower bound on the signed terminal entries
is needed for the unrestricted signed-certificate statement.

For reference, in the actual nonnegative E_theta multiplier array Lambda,
the forced cross entries are exactly

 Lambda_(i,z)=theta a_i                         (i<L),
 Lambda_(L,z)=r(kappa-1)+theta(1+r),
 Lambda_(j,L)=(kappa-1)b_j                      (j<m),
 Lambda_(z,L)=kappa-1,

with all other cross entries zero. This lists the ACTUAL interpolation
coefficients rather than a formal enlargement of their allowed signs.

## 5. Repair of the terminal leaf, with all value-flow equations retained

An empty schedule has V=0 and needs no multiplier. For one positive step u,
let c=lambda_01. Value cancellation gives lambda_10=c+2u. The constant
gradient equality puts (1,1) in the PSD kernel. Its second row equals
u(c-2)/2, so c=2. The full form is

 (2-u^2)||g_0-g_1||^2.

Thus PSD forces u<=sqrt(2), and both coefficients are already nonnegative.

For two positive steps (u,v), put s=u+v and
c=lambda_02, d=lambda_20, a=lambda_01, b=lambda_12.
Here c,b can be signed, whereas a,d,lambda_10,lambda_21 are nonnegative.
Value cancellation and the constant-gradient PSD kernel give respectively

 lambda_10=a+c-d+2u,       lambda_21=b+c-d+2s,
 u a+s c=2u,              v(b+c)+u d=2s(u+1).

For example these kernel equations follow by expanding the first and last
rows in the following six residuals, where F_ij=f_i-f_j:

 Q_01=F_01+(1/2-u)||g_0||^2+||g_1||^2/2-<g_0,g_1>,
 Q_10=F_10+(||g_0||^2+||g_1||^2)/2+(u-1)<g_0,g_1>,
 Q_12=F_12+(1/2-v)||g_1||^2+||g_2||^2/2-<g_1,g_2>,
 Q_21=F_21+(||g_1||^2+||g_2||^2)/2+(v-1)<g_1,g_2>,
 Q_02=F_02+(1/2-u)||g_0||^2+||g_2||^2/2
                                     -v<g_0,g_1>-<g_0,g_2>,
 Q_20=F_20+(||g_0||^2+||g_2||^2)/2
                                   +(u-1)<g_0,g_2>+v<g_1,g_2>.

Solving the four scalar equations gives

 a=2-(s/u)c,
 b=2s(u+1)/v-c-(u/v)d,
 lambda_10=2(u+1)-(v/u)c-d,
 b=2(u+1)+(u/v)lambda_10>0.                       (12)

Write U=g_0-g_1, Z=g_1-g_2. Since the constant gradient is in the kernel,
the entire full form is obtained by setting g_2=0 and expanding the six
displayed residuals. The result is

 [2-u^2+v(u-1)c/u]||U||^2
  +[c-(u-1)d]<U,Z>+[C_0-(u/v)d]||Z||^2,
 C_0=s[2(u+1)/v-s].                               (13)

If c>=0, all supplied coefficients are nonnegative, by (12), so retain them
and their full PSD matrix. If c<0, PSD in (13) forces C_0>=0 because d>=0.
It also forces u<=sqrt(2): this holds directly when u<1, and when u>=1 the
c contribution to the first diagonal is nonpositive, so 2-u^2>=0.
Replace the supplied array by

 lambda'_01=2, lambda'_10=2+2u,
 lambda'_12=2s(u+1)/v, lambda'_21=2s(u+1)/v+2s,
 lambda'_02=lambda'_20=0.                         (14)

Every coefficient is nonnegative, all value-flow equations remain valid,
and the full remainder, by (13) with c=d=0, is

 (2-u^2)||U||^2+C_0||Z||^2>=0.                    (15)

This deliberately changes the whole leaf matrix; no fixed-matrix repair is
claimed.

## 6. Explicit bottom-up reconstruction on arbitrarily long schedules

Repeatedly apply Sections 2-4 to the fixed decomposition chain. This extracts
ordinary certificates (lambda^a,M_a) for every earlier prefix block, and one
signed certificate for the final leaf. Apply Section 5 to that leaf.

At a cut, suppose sigma^b is the newly obtained ordinary tail array and
N_b its full PSD matrix. Define the rebuilt parent array by:

 within P: use lambda^a;
 within B: use kappa sigma^b_ij+(kappa-1)b_i 1_(i<m,j=z);
 cross: use (kappa-1)b_j on j->L for j<m,
        kappa-1 on z->L, and r(kappa-1) on L->z;
 every other cross entry: zero.                   (16)

All actual coefficients in (16) are nonnegative. Equation (8) gives, with
every value term retained,

 V_h+sum lambda_new,ij Q_ij=M_a+kappa N_b>=0.       (17)

The right side is a block-diagonal full PSD matrix on all original gradient
coordinates at this level. Repeat (16) until the root is rebuilt. The
recursion is finite because each tail is strictly shorter. This proves the
candidate theorem without a solver, a removed interpolation edge, or an
assumed tail coefficient floor.

Along multiple cuts the retained credit also has the exact telescoping law

 1-theta_leaf=(1-theta_root)(C_leaf/C_root)^2,       (18)

since each factor 1/kappa is the squared capacity ratio. This states credit
transport only; validity and repair come from the full coefficient proof.

## 7. Original-index zero steps

For a nonnegative schedule, partition query indices into classes at identical
positions caused by consecutive zero steps. Restrict the supplied full
certificate to the subspace where each such class has equal f and g. This is
a linear congruence of its PSD matrix. Between members of one class Q=0.
For different classes, aggregate all coefficients between the two classes.
Every nonterminal-target coefficient remains nonnegative. Only the terminal
class can have signed incoming coefficients. For an E_theta certificate its
aggregated lower bound is -theta times the sum of h_i in the source class,
which is the corresponding positive compressed step.

The restricted energy is exactly the positive-compressed V. Apply the theorem
to this compressed signed certificate. To lift the ordinary output, choose
the original positive-step query as representative of each nonterminal
class, and original n as terminal representative. Put each compressed
multiplier on the corresponding original pair, and all others zero. The
selected positions agree as formal identities because omitted steps have
zero coefficient. Thus the output energy and interpolation sum give the
compressed PSD quadratic on the selected independent gradients; every unused
original gradient has a zero row and column. No equality between distinct
formal gradients is imposed in the final certificate. The empty compression
has V=0 and the all-zero certificate.

## 8. An explicit unbounded family where this move changes the matrix

For any integer k>=0 take a to be k unit steps and b=(1,1). Put A=k+1,
D=3, and choose the positive root r of r(A+3+r)=3A. Let T=A+3+r and
kappa=(T/3)^2. This gives an (k+3)-positive-step schedule in R.
The prefix has the explicit ordinary adjacent certificate

 lambda^a_(i,i+1)=2(i+1)^2,
 lambda^a_(i+1,i)=2(i+1)(i+2), 0<=i<k,
 M_a=sum_(i<k)(i+1)^2||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2.

Substitution of the unit-step residuals verifies cancellation and this
identity; at query i the net flow is -2, and at query k it is 2k.

Set c=-1+1/(2kappa), so -1<c<0. On the two-unit-step tail use

 lambda^b_01=2-2c, lambda^b_10=4-c,
 lambda^b_12=8-c,  lambda^b_21=12,
 lambda^b_02=c,   lambda^b_20=0.

Its full remainder is ||U||^2+c<U,Z>+4||Z||^2, equal to
||U+(c/2)Z||^2+(4-c^2/4)||Z||^2, so it is PSD. Build a signed parent by
the formulas (16), using this signed tail instead of an ordinary tail.
The parent's only negative coefficient is

 lambda_(tail 0,z)=kappa c+(kappa-1)=-1/2.

All other nonterminal-target coefficients are nonnegative, and all other
terminal coefficients are nonnegative. It is therefore an E_(1/2)
certificate: adding h_i/2 to every incoming-terminal coefficient leaves
every actual multiplier nonnegative (the displayed negative entry becomes
exactly zero). Its full matrix is

 M_a+kappa[||U||^2+c<U,Z>+4||Z||^2].

The repair (14)-(16) yields the ordinary full matrix

 M_a+kappa[||U||^2+4||Z||^2].

The difference is -kappa c<U,Z>, an indefinite quadratic when c!=0.
Thus this is a genuine change of the PSD matrix, not the addition of an
automatically PSD term or a claim that all old coefficients already met the
ordinary floor. No claim that every possible fixed-matrix repair fails on
this example is made. No capacity gain or asymptotic improvement is claimed.

A rational calibration is k=1, giving h=(1,2,1,1), r=1, T=6, kappa=4,
c=-7/8. The ACTUAL nonnegative E_(1/2) coefficients are

 Lambda_01=2, Lambda_10=4,
 Lambda_21=Lambda_31=Lambda_41=3, Lambda_14=4,
 Lambda_23=15, Lambda_32=39/2,
 Lambda_34=39, Lambda_43=48, Lambda_04=1/2,

with every unlisted entry zero. Its full remainder is

 ||g_0-g_1||^2+4||g_2-g_3||^2
 -(7/2)<g_2-g_3,g_3-g_4>+16||g_3-g_4||^2.

The ordinary repaired coefficients are

 lambda'_01=2, lambda'_10=4,
 lambda'_21=lambda'_31=lambda'_41=lambda'_14=3,
 lambda'_23=8, lambda'_32=16,
 lambda'_34=35, lambda'_43=48, lambda'_24=3,

with every unlisted entry zero. Its full remainder is
||g_0-g_1||^2+4||g_2-g_3||^2+16||g_3-g_4||^2.
Expanding the ten possible scalar Gram monomials and five independent
function values verifies both identities exactly. A trivial rational
coefficient check reproduced these expansions; it is not the proof, an SDP
solve, or a verification-service verdict.

## 9. Exact scope and the unresolved interface

The local rigidity requires rT=AD. Without this equality, the second Huber
trajectory has energy A^2-T^2r^2/D^2 instead of zero, and it supplies neither
the two kernel vectors nor (7). The theorem therefore cannot be used to
repair a schedule with no such saturated decomposition.

Even for a saturated decomposition ending in a longer irreducible tail, the
restriction (10) only yields another signed certificate, with precisely the
increased credit (2). It is not an ordinary certificate until that remaining
tail is separately repaired. In particular, this proof does not settle a
general four-step leaf, general terminal-saturation of primal witnesses, or
the unrestricted exact B_N objective comparison. Its useful output is a
constructive, full-matrix repair on the specified class, and a narrower
remaining location for any strict cone separation.

# C96: persistent outer gates with an exact transverse cost

Status: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT, not a fact-graph result. Formal
verification is forbidden by the current allocation. The original target is
OPEN. This is the second and last allocated round (completed ceiling 27).

Accepted sources used: b98bb807d514e80d (single-cut and Huber bounds),
1fca3e5dd555cb4a (ray feasibility and objective), e9a55f57461f253c (the
stronger fixed H6 reference), and 04219554e41d9750 (the unconditional
original-denominator limit Z_1). No conditional seed from the last fact is
assumed. C93--C95 are motivation, never proof premises. All new assertions
used in this draft are proved together below.

## 1. Static model, physical word, and parameters

Fix an integer m>=6. Put lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda*(1+1/m),
beta=1-1/m, a=beta/c, and ell=1+a. Define H_0=() and

    H_(k+1)=(H_k,1+a*c^k,H_k), 0<=k<m.

Every subword uses these SAME enclosing-m parameters. Let

    j=2^(m-1)-1, n=2^m-1, P=sum H_(m-1),
    r=a*c^(m-1), b=1+r, d=1/(P+1), sigma=r*d/(2P+1),
    Kcut=r^2*d^2/(2P+1),
    p=12/13, q=5/13, rho=q^2/p^2=25/144,
    kappa=1/ell, J=1+rho*ell, w=1/(2p^2)=169/288.

The symbol Kcut is the accepted central single-cut value on this exact word;
it is not the entire worst-case functional W_n and need not equal the Huber
value. Choose dimensionless constants

    0<epsilon<=1/4, |eta|<=1/4.

For positive Rminus,Rplus define phi(0)=0 and
phi'(s)=clip(s,[-Rminus,Rplus]). Explicitly phi(s)=s^2/2 in the core,
phi(s)=Rplus*s-Rplus^2/2 on the positive outer interval, and
phi(s)=-Rminus*s-Rminus^2/2 on the negative outer interval.

Use u_+=(p,q), u_-=(p,-q), positive radii

    Rplus_+=p*d*(1+epsilon), Rplus_-=p*d*(1-epsilon),
    Rminus_+=p*sigma*(1+eta), Rminus_-=p*sigma*(1-eta),

and the corresponding two functions phi_+,phi_-. Define ONE static function

    F(x,y)=kappa*y^2/2+w*phi_+(p*x+q*y)+w*phi_-(p*x-q*y).

Run actual GD from X_0=(1,0) using L=1 and exactly the word H_m. There is
no objective switch, stepsize adaptation, or local curvature rescaling.

## 2. Global legality and the actual minimizer

The useful uniform parameter bounds are

    2/7<a<sqrt(2)-1<1/2, 9/7<ell<3/2,
    0<kappa<7/9, 0<rho<1/5, 1<J<13/10,
    P>31, r>9, b>10, b/r<10/9.                     (C96.1)

Indeed a=(m-1)/((m+1)*lambda)>=5/(7lambda)>2/7 because
lambda<5/2; also a<1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1. There are at least 31 steps in
H_(m-1), all greater than one. Finally c>2 gives
r>(2/7)*2^5=64/7>9. The remaining inequalities follow by substitution.

For any two scalar arguments, the difference of a clip is theta times
the difference of those arguments, where 0<=theta<=1. Thus the difference
of gradients at X,Y equals D(X-Y), where

    D=diag(0,kappa)+w*theta_+*u_+*u_+^T+w*theta_-*u_-*u_-^T,
    0<=D<=C=diag(1,kappa+rho)<=diag(1,137/144)<=I.

The Euclidean norm of D is at most one, proving global 1-smoothness.
Each summand is convex, proving convexity of F. Every summand is
nonnegative. Since the two nonparallel ridge vectors span R^2 and each
phi vanishes only at zero, argmin F={0} and min F=0. Consequently the
true initial squared distance is exactly one and the payoff is V=2F(X_n).

The quadratic background is singular, so it has no positive curvature
floor on the full active plane. The total core matrix C is not I. These
properties follow from exact algebraic inequalities, not rounded clipping.

## 3. Exact prefix and its central queried gate

Write X_i=(x_i,y_i). Put

    delta=epsilon*p*d, zeta=eta*p*sigma,
    y_plus=-epsilon*(q/p)*d*ell,
    y_minus=eta*(q/p)*sigma*ell,
    Lcut=epsilon*d*J/2.

As long as both ridges are in the positive outer intervals, the gradient is

    grad F=(d,kappa*y+delta*q/p^2).

The FIRST physical step is ell=1/kappa. It sends y_0=0 exactly to y_plus,
the stationary value of this affine scalar recursion. Every subsequent
step preserves y_plus until a ridge changes gate, irrespective of its size.
The proposed prefix is therefore

    x_i=1-d*sum_(t<i) h_t,
    y_0=0, y_i=y_plus for 1<=i<=j.                 (C96.2)

Here is an induction validating every proposed gate, rather than assuming
the affine formula remains applicable. At i=0 both projections are p, while
Rplus_+<=5p/(4(P+1))<p. For 1<=i<j, the remaining prefix mass includes its
last step ell, so x_i-d>=ell*d. Hence

    p*x_i+q*y_plus-Rplus_+
       >=p*d*(ell-epsilon*J)>0.

The other projection is even larger than p*x_i, and Rplus_-<p*d. This
proves that both ridges remain strictly positive outer before index j.
Thus (C96.2) follows from the ACTUAL recursion, and x_j=d.

At index j the first ridge projection is

    p*d+q*y_plus=p*d*(1-epsilon*(J-1)).

It is positive because epsilon*(J-1)<3/40, and strictly less than
Rplus_+. It therefore lies strictly in that ridge's core. The second
projection is p*d*(1+epsilon*(J-1))>Rplus_-, so the second ridge is still
positive outer. Direct differentiation now gives

    grad F(X_j)=(d-Lcut,-(q/p)*Lcut),
    x_(j+1)=-r*d+b*Lcut,
    y_(j+1)=epsilon*(q/p)*d*(b*J/2-ell).          (C96.3)

The last y-coordinate is positive by b>10 and ell<3/2. In particular the
central gradient is noncollinear with the stationary precentral gradient
(d,0). The actual gate Hessians diag(0,kappa) and
diag(0,kappa)+w*u_+*u_+^T do not commute: their off-diagonal commutator
has nonzero magnitude kappa*w*p*q.

## 4. Both ridges stay negative outer throughout the full suffix

First verify the point immediately after the central step. By (C96.1),

    x_(j+1)+(q/p)*abs(y_(j+1))
      <=d*(-r+b*epsilon*J*(1+rho)/2)
      <d*(-r+39*b/200)
      <-47*r*d/60.

Since 2P+1>63 and |eta|<=1/4, this is strictly below

    -sigma*(1+|eta|) >= -5*r*d/(4*(2P+1)).

More explicitly, 47/60>5/(4*(2P+1)), so the last strict comparison has
the required orientation. Both ridge projections are therefore below
their own negative thresholds at X_(j+1).

On the negative outer intersection the full gradient is

    grad F=(-sigma,kappa*y-q*zeta/p^2).

The FIRST step of the right H_(m-1) block is again ell. It sets y exactly
to y_minus. Subsequent steps preserve that value as long as the gates
stay negative outer, while x increases monotonically. The proposed terminal
point is

    x_n=-sigma*(P+1)+b*Lcut, y_n=y_minus.         (C96.4)

To validate all the proposed post-first-step suffix gates it suffices to
check the largest proposed x, namely x_n. The inequality

    x_n+(q/p)*abs(y_minus)<-sigma*(1+|eta|)

is equivalent to

    P-|eta|*J > (b/r)*(2P+1)*epsilon*J/2.        (C96.5)

The left side exceeds P-13/40. The right side is less than
13*(2P+1)/72. Their difference exceeds
(23/36)*P-13/40-13/72>0, because P>31. This proves (C96.5).
Induction now verifies the complete actual suffix, including its unqueried
terminal point. It does not replace physical steps by a Hessian-dependent
schedule. Every one of the slanted ridges remains strictly negative outer
at every postcentral query and at the terminal point.

This trajectory NEVER visits the common core intersection. In particular,
it refutes an unconditional extension of eventual common-core capture to
all singular-background, nonsaturated-core models. Its poor objective
cannot be attributed to entering the common quadratic region.

## 5. Exact objective and a whole two-parameter family obstruction

When both ridges are negative outer, expanding the defining static function
gives

    F(x,y)=-sigma*x-(q*zeta/p^2)*y
           -(sigma^2+zeta^2/p^2)/2+kappa*y^2/2.

No function-value offsets are deleted. At y=y_minus=q*zeta/(p^2*kappa),
the transverse linear and quadratic terms combine to
-q^2*zeta^2/(2p^4*kappa). Substituting (C96.4) yields

    V=2F(X_n)
     =Kcut-2*sigma*b*Lcut-zeta^2*J/p^2
     =Kcut*(1-epsilon*b*J/r-eta^2*J/(2P+1)).    (C96.6)

This is the main joint statement: for EVERY m>=6 and EVERY allowed
epsilon,eta the single static two-ridge function above is globally legal,
has the stated actual minimizer and queried itinerary, and obeys (C96.6).
The proof is entirely symbolic and independent of finite trajectory runs.

The payoff is strictly smaller than Kcut because epsilon>0. V is positive,
since the terminal point is strictly in the negative outer region and is
not the unique minimizer. As an algebraic check, epsilon*b*J/r<13/36
and eta^2*J/(2P+1)<13/10080, so the displayed bracket is positive as well.

Accepted b98bb807d514e80d gives Kcut<=K_n(H_m), and accepted
1fca3e5dd555cb4a gives K_n(H_m)<=F_ray_n(H_m)<=W_n(H_m). Consequently
this entire explicit changing-region family fails to improve even the
accepted central single cut, for all horizons in its stated domain. A
negative tilt eta cannot repair the loss: it incurs the exact additional
quadratic price in (C96.6). This is not an upper bound on unrestricted W_n.

The same conclusion prices a free initial transverse displacement as long
as the initial point (1,y0) lies in both positive outer regions. Its first
step sends y0 to the same y_plus, so all later states and terminal values
are unchanged. Its TRUE initial squared distance is 1+y0^2; its normalized
payoff is the expression in (C96.6) divided by 1+y0^2. A large transverse
initial coordinate cannot be silently removed from the denominator.

## 6. A scalable law on the ORIGINAL enclosing-m sequence

Fix epsilon,eta in the displayed ranges independently of m. Let
M_k=1+sum H_k and r_k=a*c^k. From the physical word,

    M_0=1, M_(k+1)=2M_k+r_k,
    M_k=2^k+a*(c^k-2^k)/(c-2).                 (C96.7)

Here c,a still depend on m. As m tends to infinity, c tends to lambda,
a tends to 1/lambda=lambda-2, (2/c)^(m-1) tends to zero, and therefore
M_(m-1)/c^(m-1) tends to 1. Also (1+1/m)^(m-1) tends to exp(1). Thus

    r/(P+1) -> 1/lambda,
    lambda^m*Kcut -> 1/(2*exp(1)*lambda),
    J -> 1+rho*sqrt(2), b/r -> 1.

The second limit follows directly by writing
Kcut=[r/(P+1)]^2/(2M_(m-1)-1). In (C96.6), the eta term tends to zero.
We obtain the fully priced nonzero limit

    lambda^m*V ->
      [1-epsilon*(1+rho*sqrt(2))]/[2*exp(1)*lambda].   (C96.8)

The unconditional portion of accepted fact 04219554e41d9750 states that
Z_1=lim_m lambda^(-m)*B_(2^m-1) exists and is finite and positive, for the
ORIGINAL all-split benchmark denominator. Hence

    B_(2^m-1)*V ->
      Z_1*[1-epsilon*(1+rho*sqrt(2))]/[2*exp(1)*lambda]. (C96.9)

Only that accepted unconditional limit is used; no seed assumption or
conditional separation from the same fact is imported. Formula (C96.9)
is the payoff of this exhibited family, not the true worst-case value.

For comparison with the same-word global Huber witness, S=sum H_m and
M_m=1+S. Equations (C96.7) give lambda^m/(1+2S)->1/(2*exp(1)), so

    V / [1/(1+2S)] ->
      [1-epsilon*(1+rho*sqrt(2))]/lambda < 1.

No finite m=6,7,8 observation is used to prove these limits. Conversely
none of those finite cases is described as a test of an m>=128 theorem.

## 7. Bounded discovery, reconstruction, and failure accounting

The round started from the existing ledger at 16 charged cases, 14 discovery
cases, one calibration, one exact reconstruction, and 0.6265509749991907
measured/accounted seconds. Calibration against the accepted scalar central
cut had already passed in case1; it was not rerun or treated as free.

Cases17--22 weakened or removed the vertical ridge in the previous rational
paired-ridge models. All six returned finite trajectories and changed queried
gates; none exceeded K. Cases23--28 replaced that vertical ridge by a singular
quadratic background with rational coefficient. All six also returned finite
trajectories and no gain. The largest successful new discovery ratio was
case23 at approximately 0.8972369014212274*K. Its background has a zero
horizontal eigenvalue, so no uniform positive background floor is presumed.

Case29 was a CHARGED FAILED proposal. Its exact algebraic-curvature prefilter
raised TypeError: "'>=' not supported between instances of 'Q2' and 'int'".
The raw error, request/source hashes, and public launch event are retained.
The run stopped after that error. Defining the missing exact comparison
operators repaired the implementation; separately charged case30 retried
the same proposed static function. No accounting was reset or erased.

Case30 uses epsilon=1/12, eta=0 in the family above. Case31 uses
epsilon=eta=1/12. Case32 uses epsilon=1/4, eta=0. Their numerical V/K ratios
were respectively approximately 0.8959721668863, 0.8959216103034, and
0.6879165006589. Their actual two-ridge itineraries are exactly the proposed
central chronology, with changes at indices 31,32 for ridge1 and 32 for
ridge2. Both terminal ridges are negative outer. No finite gain is claimed.

Broad discovery stopped after these cases. The final two reserves were used
ONLY for exact reconstruction of the SAME static objects as cases30 and31:
cases33 and34 respectively. They preserve the original m=6 physical steps,
algebraic kappa, rational directions and weights, all radii definitions, and
initial point. Exact Q(sqrt(2)) arithmetic used rational-square sign tests;
decimal conversion was only for readable outputs. Both runs checked every
actual gate, every sampled value/gradient/point, the central and terminal
coordinate formulas, and the EXACT identity (C96.6), including its strict
comparison with the central cut. Neither is a verifier verdict.

The cumulative ledger before closure has 34 charged cases: 30 discovery
(including the one charged error), one calibration, and three exact
reconstructions. There are 33 finite raw statuses and one error. The two
remaining discovery slots are retired on allocation closure; reserves are
fully used. No solver, installation, formal verifier call, C59 replay, or
benchmark dynamic program was run. Final metered seconds and hashes are
recorded in the companion manifest after closure, not estimated here.

## 8. Same-word references and interpretation

All forward results retain the global Huber term, every single-cut value,
the best single-cut index, and the sufficient first-two-ray comparison on
the SAME physical word. The complete finite symbolic argument showing
F_ray=K for m=6,7,8 is reproduced as an integrated appendix below. It is
joint draft mathematics, not an invocation of unverified C95 as a fact.

For m=6 the stronger accepted reference is e9a55f57461f253c:
W_63(H6)>56/2711>1/50. The exhibited root family is far smaller even by
an elementary comparison. If t_k=r_k/M_k, then
t_(k+1)=c*t_k/(2+t_k). Since c>lambda, one has
c*(c-2)>lambda*(lambda-2)=1>beta and hence a<c-2. Also
c<=7*lambda/6<35/12<3. Thus a<c-2<1, and induction gives t_k<c-2<1,
so Kcut<1/(2P+1)<1/63<1/50. The C59 reference is not transplanted to
different enclosing-m words, and none of its 4160 checks was replayed.

This work supplies a concrete scoped obstruction and a useful exact relation.
Persistent outer gates, a singular background, a nonsaturated core matrix,
and a noncommuting central gate do not by themselves yield improved payoff.
The decisive restriction in the explicit solved family is the reciprocal
short-step coefficient, which kills the transverse transient at the first
step of each child. General coefficients retain a transverse polynomial
and are outside this exact family theorem. General multilevel gate changes,
unequal ridge directions/weights, and arbitrary static models remain open.

The general coefficient relation, useful for a future authorized round, is
as follows. Retain a fixed kappa>0 and the same symmetric directions/weights
and radii means. Suppose the ACTUAL chronology is both plus before the
central query, core/plus there, and both minus afterwards. Let
R=product_(h in H_(m-1))(1-kappa*h), delta=(Rplus_+-Rplus_-)/2,
zeta=(Rminus_+-Rminus_-)/2, and

    yj=-delta*q*(1-R)/(p^2*kappa),
    L=(delta-q*yj)/(2p),
    yc=[1-b*(kappa+rho/2)]*yj-b*delta*q/(2p^2),
    ye=q*zeta/(p^2*kappa),
    xn=-r*d+b*L+P*sigma, yn=ye+(yc-ye)*R.

Solving the two affine recursions and differentiating the central core gate
gives these formulas directly. Substitution into the exact static value gives

    V=-2*sigma*xn-2*q*zeta*yn/p^2-sigma^2-zeta^2/p^2+kappa*yn^2.

Unlike (C96.6), this last interface is CONDITIONAL on its stated complete
gate itinerary. It is not a construction certificate for arbitrary kappa.
The exact root family proves that itinerary separately in Sections 3--4.

The original arbitrary-schedule exact-profile problem remains unresolved.
No lower witness on H_m, even if stronger, would alone disprove minimax
optimality: that negative goal needs an improving schedule's full-class
upper certificate.

## Appendix: complete same-word finite scalar comparison

This appendix is included in the present joint derivation. It has no
unverified predecessor. Here K is the accepted functional K_n(H_m), and
F_ray is the complete accepted family from fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a.

Here is a direct sufficient test for the complete accepted ray family to
equal K. For i<j put P=sum_(t<i)h_t and D=sum_(i<t<j)h_t. If

    (h_i-1)(h_j-1)<(1+P)(1+D)                 (R)

for every i<j, no feasible accepted ray selection has two checkpoints.
Indeed its first two checkpoint gradients are
m_0=1/(1+P), m_1=(h_i-1)m_0/(1+D), with old offset P*m_0.
The second-return budget is (h_j-1)m_1-P*m_0<m_0 by (R).
With exactly two checkpoints the old-gradient requirement is at least
m_0, and with more checkpoints the third magnitude would be below m_0.
Both contradict the explicit feasibility conditions of 1fca3e5dd555cb4a.
Zero and one checkpoint produce exactly K. Any numerical check of (R)
will be labeled numerical until an exact symbolic bound is supplied.



Write r_j=a*c^j and M_j=1+sum H_j. Then

    M_0=1, M_(j+1)=2*M_j+r_j,
    t_j=r_j/M_j, t_0=a, t_(j+1)=c*t_j/(2+t_j).

For m>=6, c>1+sqrt(2) and beta<1 imply a<c-2. Hence t_j increases
and stays below c-2. Also

    beta-(c-2) = 3-(1+sqrt(2))-(2+sqrt(2))/m
       >= (10-7*sqrt(2))/6 >0,

so t_j>=a>(c-2)/c.

For any two chronological indices i<j with levels u,v, define the ratio
R_ij in (R) with the right side as its denominator. If u>=v, the prefix
before i contains H_u and the gap before j contains H_v; thus
R_ij<=t_u*t_v. The largest level m-1 occurs only once, so at least one
of u,v is at most m-2. It follows that

    R_ij<=t_(m-1)*t_(m-2)<c*t_(m-2)^2.

If u<v, both the prefix before i and the gap from i to j contain H_u.
Their total mass is at least sum H_v-(1+r_u), since the prefix before
j contains H_v. Consequently, putting x=1+P and y=1+D,
x,y>=M_u and x+y>=M_v-r_u. From
(x-M_u)(y-M_u)>=0 we obtain

    x*y>=M_u*(M_v-M_u-r_u).

Let d=v-u>=1 and t=t_u. Directly iterating the recurrence for M gives

    (M_v-M_u-r_u)/M_u
      =2^d-1+t*((c^d-2^d)/(c-2)-1)
      >=c^(d-1)+(c-1)*(2^d-2)/c>=c^(d-1).

The first inequality uses t>=(c-2)/c and the nonnegative coefficient
((c^d-2^d)/(c-2)-1). Therefore R_ij<=c*t_u^2<=c*t_(m-2)^2.
Equality in the complete maximum occurs at the first level-(m-2) bridge
and the immediately following level-(m-1) bridge: both intervening
prefix/gap masses are sum H_(m-2). Thus

    max_(i<j) R_ij = c*t_(m-2)^2.                         (A1)

This proves the full pair test symbolically; it does not rely on sampled
pair scans. Here are short rational upper certificates for the three
authorized horizons. Monotonicity of c*t/(2+t) in both positive variables
allows use of the displayed upper parameters throughout.

* m=6: c<17/6 and a<3/10. Four recurrence steps give
  t_4<83521/144462<7/12. Thus c*t_4^2<833/864<1.
  The intermediate bounds are 17/46,289/654,4913/9582,83521/144462.
* m=7: c<83/30 and a<5/16. Bounds for t_1,...,t_5 are respectively
  3/8,7/16,1/2,83/150,3/5. For the last step,
  6889/11490<3/5; hence c*t_5^2<747/750<1.
* m=8: c<68/25 and a<13/40. The upper recurrence has reciprocal at
  its sixth step equal to
  25/18+(395/234)*(25/34)^6. This is greater than 33/20 because
  (25/34)^3>79/200 and (79/200)^2>611/3950. Therefore t_6<20/33,
  and c*t_6^2<27200/27225<1.

All parameter enclosures follow by squaring positive rational bounds for
sqrt(2). In particular sqrt(2)<10/7, sqrt(2)>29/21 prove the m=6
bounds; sqrt(2)<341/240 and sqrt(2)>7/5 prove m=7;
sqrt(2)<319/225 and sqrt(2)>163/117 prove m=8.
Combining (A1) with the accepted ray feasibility conditions proves

    F_ray_(2^m-1)(H_m)=K_(2^m-1)(H_m), m=6,7,8.           (A2)

No m>=128 result is used here. This finite symbolic comparison is part of the present UNVERIFIED joint
draft; it is not an accepted graph fact.

For completeness the largest single-cut term is the central cut. For a
fixed level u put p=1+P, q=1+T; both are at least M_u and
p+q=M_m-r_u. The denominator p^2*(2q-1) has no interior minimum on
that interval: its derivative is 2p*(2*(M_m-r_u)-3p-1), with at most
one interior critical point, a maximum. The two endpoint denominators
are ordered by monotonicity of z^2/(2z-1) for z>=1, so the earliest
occurrence of level u is best. Its value is

    t_u^2/[2*M_m-2*M_u-2*r_u-1].

The numerator increases and the positive denominator decreases with u.
Thus the central level m-1 maximizes it. In particular K is exactly
the larger of the global Huber term and the central single-cut term;
no choice of untested checkpoints is hidden in the numerical reference.

# Same circular function: terminal values, positive-charge overcount, and precision failure

Status: NUMERICAL DISCOVERY ONLY. No fact was submitted or accepted. The
original all-word/all-horizon problem and the asymptotic middle-component
question remain OPEN. The one allocated max3 round after baseline61 is
finished; expected completed count62, ceiling62/attempted63. The official
post-return counter has not been observed. No automatic renewal.

The most useful observation is at m16 with independently recomputed
65-significant-digit decimal arithmetic: all four circular terminal values
were numerically zero, while the original accumulated X_m was positive at
the original benchmark scale. The full terminal objective was carried by
the terminal Huber and agreed with its source formula to relative error
3.8445684035925723e-44. This is finite-depth evidence that X_m can count
activity later dissipated. It is not an exact zero, a certified lower or
upper bound, or an asymptotic conclusion.

A second essential result is a numerical reliability failure. The complete
binary64 m16 and m20 histories both ended at a numerically zero objective,
and both had relative error 1 against their positive terminal Huber formula.
The independent m16 decimal evolution differed from binary64 by as much as
0.1244610627244608 in one coordinate. Local projection KKT checks passed
throughout both evolutions. Thus small local residuals do not certify the
trajectory's accuracy through these large steps.

## What was frozen and executed

The full evidence of source findings84a4435a88a9f487 and d5345c6e59f22ce3
was read. These remain joint unverified drafts, not accepted predecessors.
The construction was preserved exactly as a mathematical specification:
k=floor(sqrt(m)), beta_arc=1/k, eta_word=1-1/(4m),
c=(1+sqrt(2))*(1+1/(4m)), a=eta_word/c; the first occurrences of levels
m-k,...,m-1 in the same enclosing-m physical ruler word; the full circular
convex body; all offsets from the exact local backward rule; the static
half-sum of adjacent circular envelopes and terminal half-Huber; x_0=e_1.
All actual old and future component gradients remained in every update.

The pre-execution derivation and all six semantic slots are in
preregistration.md, appended verbatim to the complete shared evidence.
They include the circle's center and radius, exact Euclidean projection,
full-circle support function, global gradient and half factors, backward
rule, true closest radius, exact short calibration, diagnostics, raw archive
formats, benchmark directions and resource limits. No finite-plane
surrogate, extra component, state clipping, independent checkpoint reset,
mass-only forward replacement, or unallocated mathematical case was used.

Source discovery.py SHA256:
e4b4084cf8741418dbb89e772682047f82f9508d800b96d04f274397c61b05df.

aef12f0500b4cbf4b25eee3cbb660a1d2c2cfde4918fe744ad59646214dcce4d.

Decimal m16: arithmetic context radix10, precision65 significant decimal

## Terminal values and the original benchmark

# Appendix: complete preregistration frozen before numerical execution

# Frozen discovery specification, round 62

This is numerical discovery, with no fact submission. Source findings
84a4435a88a9f487 and d5345c6e59f22ce3 were read in full. Their mathematical
status remains joint unverified. No asymptotic theorem is asserted at the
finite depths below. All old allocations remain closed.

## Static function and exact formulas, before execution

For each enclosing m in {16,20,25}, set k=floor(sqrt(m)), J=m-k,
beta=1/k, lambda=1+sqrt(2), eta_word=1-1/(4m),
c=lambda*(1+1/(4m)), a=eta_word/c. Every child word uses THESE parameters.
The physical step at zero-based index t is b_v2(t+1), with
b_j=1+a*c^j; H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j). The length is n=2^m-1.
The selected indices are 2^(J+i)-1 for i=0,...,k-1. The ordinary block
levels are J,J,J+1,...,m-2. Their masses obey S_0=0,
S_(j+1)=2S_j+b_j. The final suffix starts at query 2^(m-1).

Write C=beta+sqrt(1+2beta), R_circle=1+beta, kap=1+beta^2.
In coordinates (p,-q), the FULL convex body is
K={0<=q<=1, beta*q<=p, (p-beta)^2+(q+beta)^2<=R_circle^2}.
Its boundary consists of the segment from 0 to (C,0), the segment
from 0 to (beta,-1), and the circular arc connecting their far endpoints.
The displayed disk intersects those two half-planes in exactly the
horizontal sections beta*q<=p<=beta+sqrt(R_circle^2-(q+beta)^2).

For any centered normalized input w=(r,s), its projection is w if
w belongs to K. Otherwise a nearest boundary point is among:
(clip(r,0,C),0); (beta*t,-t), t=clip((beta*r-s)/kap,0,1);
and the radial point (beta,beta)+R_circle*(w-(beta,beta))/||w-(beta,beta)||
when that point lies on the stated arc. These are the exact nearest
points on the two segments and on the arc whenever the latter has an
interior stationary minimum; remaining arc minima are endpoints already
included. Choose the candidate maximizing <g,w>-||g||^2/2.
The production implementation can return the radial disk projection
directly when it belongs to the arc, since K is a subset of that disk.
For s>=0 the horizontal projection is exact. For r<=0 the diagonal
projection is exact, by maximizing first over U=p-beta*q>=0.
These are projection calculations, never projections of the GD state.

The full support function at d=(d_x,d_y) is the maximum of
0, C*d_x, beta*d_x-d_y, and
beta*(d_x+d_y)+R_circle*sqrt(d_x^2+d_y^2) if d_x>0, d_y<0 and
(-d_y)*sqrt(1+2beta)>=beta*d_x. This is obtained by maximizing the
linear functional along the entire arc; the derivative has a unique
interior maximum in precisely that angular sector. The endpoints pay
all other directions, including d_x=0. Thus projection KKT is checked
against the whole convex hull by h_K(w-g)-<w-g,g>, not sampled arc points.

For an ordinary block of level j, construct its local data backwards
from u=beta,y=1. Reading the actual reversed physical step h, alpha=h/2,
let z=max(beta/sqrt(1+2beta),(y+(1-alpha)*beta)/(u-(1-alpha)*beta)).
If z takes the first branch use q=0,p=C; otherwise use
q=(1+beta)*z/sqrt(1+z^2)-beta, p=beta+(1+beta)/sqrt(1+z^2).
Then u<-u+alpha*p, y<-y+alpha*q. These are exactly the frozen source's
backward rule, with no asymptotic M approximation.

The sole permitted aggregation is the invariant capped backward prefix:
once q=0, y<=beta*(u-C)/sqrt(1+2beta). Further backward steps increase u
by h*C/2 and keep y unchanged, preserving that inequality. A remaining
prefix of r steps has exactly floor(r/2^l)-floor(r/2^(l+1)) occurrences
of level l. Its EXACT mass is the sum of these counts times b_l.
Only this already derived invariant prefix can be aggregated. Save every
count and the mass used; distinguish explicit backward steps from these
implicit steps. No forward update may be aggregated.

For component i=1,...,k, let u_i,y_i be these backward data and
M_i=u_i+y_i/beta. Start ell_1=0,D_1=1 and set
sigma_i=D_i/M_i, ell_(i+1)=sigma_i*y_i,
A_i=ell_i+ell_(i+1)/beta,
D_(i+1)=sigma_i*(b_(J+i-1)/2-1).
Set delta=D_(k+1)/(1+S_(m-1)). The one frozen function is
F(x)=(1/2)*sum_i E_(sigma_i*K)(x_i-A_i,x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1))
     +(1/2)*Hub_delta(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1)), starting at e_1.
Here E_C(w)=max_(g in C)(<g,w>-||g||^2/2), and Hub_delta is
0 for t<=0, t^2/2 for 0<t<delta, and delta*t-delta^2/2 above delta.
All half factors are physical. Projection scaling gives
(P_i,-Q_i)=sigma_i*Pi_K((x_i-A_i)/sigma_i,
                       (x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1))/sigma_i).
Let P_(k+1)=clip(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1),0,delta), Q_0=0.
The entire gradient is (P_i-Q_(i-1))/2, including all old/future terms.
Every actual step is x<-x-h*gradient. No state clipping, checkpoint
replacement, independent suffix initialization, or face replay is allowed.

Completing the square in E proves its gradient is its Euclidean
projection. Projection optimality gives the firmly nonexpansive inequality,
so E is convex and 1-smooth. Stacking adjacent pairs and the final scalar
counts coordinates at most twice; the half-sum F is convex and 1-smooth.
Its minimum set is x_i<=A_i, x_(i+1)>=beta*(x_i-ell_i), and
x_(k+1)<=ell_(k+1). The point x_star=(A_1,ell_2,0,...,0) is feasible.
Since 1-A_1=sigma_1*u_1, ell_2=beta*A_1=sigma_1*y_1, and
u_1>=beta*y_1, we have A_1<=1/(1+beta^2). For x_1<0 the initial
distance is at least 1; on 0<=x_1<=A_1 its square is bounded below
by (1-x_1)^2+beta^2*x_1^2, decreasing on this interval and attained
by x_star. Therefore the TRUE radius, used in every display, is
R_m^2=(1-A_1)^2+ell_2^2=(u_1^2+y_1^2)/M_1^2.

The actual terminal numerator 2F is computed from every component.
Independently compare the Huber term with D_(k+1)^2/[2(1+S_(m-1))].
During the ACTUAL last suffix accumulate, without clipping U_i=P_i-beta*Q_i,
X_flux=(1/2)*sum_t h_t sum_(i<k) sigma_i*(theta*U_i+U_(i+1)),
theta=(1-beta)/(1+beta), and at the terminal add
X_terminal=C*sum_i sigma_i*max(x_i-A_i,0).
The positive part here is the definition of X, not a change to the state.

## Six semantic slots, frozen before execution

1. Calibration, including construction of m16 constants. Exactly four
   local normalized cases at beta=1/4, sigma=1:
   (a) w=(0,-17/32), projection=(1/8,-1/2), envelope=17/128;
   (b) w=(1/4,-1/4), projection=w, envelope=1/16;
   (c) w=(2C,0), projection=(C,0), envelope=3C^2/2;
   (d) w=(9/4,-5/4), projection=(5/4,-1/2), envelope=81/32.
   One short whole-function trajectory: the first TWO m16 updates,
   with h=b_0,b_1. Preregistered exact coordinates are
   x_t=(1-C*sigma_1*sum_(s<t)h_s/2,0,...,0), t=0,1,2.
   At each of these queries the symbolic cap hypothesis is
   u_1-C*sum_(s<t)h_s/2>=C+sqrt(1+2beta)*y_1/beta.
   It must be checked and must pass, otherwise calibration fails.
   Gradient=C*sigma_1*e_1/2;
   F=(C*sigma_1*(x_1-A_1)-(C*sigma_1)^2/2)/2.
   Check x_star, its zero objective/gradient, minimum-set feasibility,
   and both true-radius formulas. No compiler is installed; source
   identity and Python runtime data replace an executable compiler manifest.
2. Full binary64 m16, k=4, n=65535. Save EVERY query 0 through n,
   including coordinates, full gradient, projections, component objectives,
   and full objective. All 65535 updates must be executed.
3. Full binary64 m20, k=4, n=1048575. Save stride 16 queries (65536
   ordinary samples), plus all selected pre/post checkpoints and terminal.
4. Full binary64 m25, k=5, n=33554431. Save stride 512 queries (65536
   ordinary samples), plus the same special queries. This slot may be
   retired if the installed runtime cannot support it in the fixed cap.
5. Independently evolve m16 using standard-library Decimal at precision
   65 significant decimal digits, round-half-even. Recompute sqrt(2), all
   word parameters, backward data, offsets and projections in that context.
   Compare EVERY query against the saved binary64 fields. Binary states
   and faces never drive this evolution. Save all aggregate errors plus
   stride 1024 decimal samples, selected checkpoints, terminal and bounded
   error extremizers. Full decimal states are NOT archived.
6. Independently coded projection/gradient/objective/radius check of ALL
   SAVED ordinary and special samples from the largest completed case.
   It uses the explicit candidate maximization formula rather than the
   production projection's region shortcuts. It performs no new trajectory.
   Alternatively this slot is the sole correction of one failed job, with
   the exact source change recorded; it cannot also perform readback.

Each job reserves 50 accounted seconds BEFORE numerical imports or model
construction, and is externally killed after 49.5 seconds to remain within
the 50-second whole-job cap. No numerical code runs outside these slots.
Failure and timeout consume their slot. No seventh job or automatic retry.
After failed calibration only the sole correction is available; if that
does not succeed, retire all unused slots and close.

All diagnostics run INSIDE the corresponding charged process. They count
every projection, update, local backward update, and diagnostic query.
Full query coverage of feasibility and KKT includes terminal queries.
Save component face counts and transitions, last activity, U minima,
positive input excursions and their largest values, last cap excursions,
first/middle/last terminal contributions, X, true radius and Huber comparison.
Feasibility and normalized KKT failure tolerance is 5e-12 for binary64
and 1e-55 for Decimal. No mathematical conclusion is certified by this
tolerance. Save every failure; stop with an incomplete run if 10000 failure
records are reached, keeping all recorded failures. No clipping repairs.
At most 4+2k diagnostic extremizer states are retained in a separate archive.
Larger binary archives are explicitly SAMPLED, never a full replay claim.
Output is limited to 128 MiB per job; fixed binary records keep ordinary
archives well below that cap. A bounded progress record permits honest
partial-count reporting on timeout.

For benchmark displays only the accepted saved table's b=1,j=11 endpoints
are used. Its SHA256 is
2a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.
With D80=1208925819614629174706176,
L2047=46381188529515973069682150122,
H2048=46409982540259750875314341333, and m>=11,
lambda^(m-11)*L2047/D80 <= B_(2^m-1)
 <= lambda^(m-11)*(sqrt(H2048/D80)+3/100)^2.
The lower direction follows from doubling monotonicity; the upper from
the accepted uniform ray enclosure. The hypotheses use the actual physical
index 2^m-1. Floating displays are NOT certified intervals. No new DP,
asymptotic finite-depth assertion, exact lower witness, or upper proof.

On closure only stored-output reading, symbolic interpretation, hashing and
administrative reporting are allowed. Unused credits are retired.
