# C106: signed terminal affine costs, the actual minimum, and a broader obstruction

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT. This is the final already allocated max3
round, completed ceiling37 / attempted ceiling38. No fact is asserted.
No numerical work, solver, machine algebra, formal verification, consult,
installation or native subagent has been used. The original Goal is OPEN.

The original target retains last iterate, one static convex L-smooth
function per run, every finite dimension, true nearest-minimizer radius,
all nonnegative predetermined words, and the W=2R normalization. It asks
for the uniform exact asymptotic Zhang--Jiang profile, or a certified fixed
relative asymptotic upper improvement for the full function class.
The bounds below concern a declared adversary family only.

The assigned zero-terminal-cost obtuse family is closed at joint-draft
level by the complete argument reproduced as Appendix J. Its source
finding7f98a959eb67c405 is retrieval evidence, NOT a correctness premise.
Every mathematical argument used from that draft is included here and
asserted jointly. The accepted inputs are only1252b8823bb5a290 (rows and
normalization), b98bb807d514e80d (complete K and original recurrences),
1fca3e5dd555cb4a (complete ray reference), and dfde2fa326c908bf (complex
and lens reference). Their relevant proofs were read in this round.

For each enclosing integer m, put lambda=1+sqrt2, beta_m=1-1/m,
c_m=lambda(1+1/m), a_m=beta_m/c_m, H_0=(), and
H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_m c_m^j,H_j) for j<m. Every child uses these SAME
enclosing-m parameters. The original word H_m has length n=2^m-1.
Let M_j=1+sum H_j. The symbols K_n,F_ray_n,B_n retain exactly the
definitions of the cited accepted facts; the full same-word comparison
and original all-split B_n recurrence are reproduced in Appendix J.

MAIN SCOPED RESULT. Permit an arbitrary real final-plane cost tau in
the family (D1) below, keeping the original independent nonnegative
middle residuals and middle cost. Derive its actual minimum level mu
and upper right minimum vertex (mu,y_plus) by (D3). Impose every
physical plane test (D2). In the ENTIRE declared subfamily satisfying

    H_c := M v + beta_o + y_plus >= 0,                 (D0)

the supremum V_m^lower, over ALL parameters and ALL original H_m
checkpoint pairs, satisfies

    limsup V_m^lower / K_(2^m-1)(H_m) <= 13/10,
    limsup B_(2^m-1) V_m^lower <= 13/[5 exp(1)] < 1.

The COMPLETE same-word K equals COMPLETE F_ray. The full Huber-augmented
universal-lens reference and the full Huber-augmented word-dependent
complex reference are each at least this K; the same13/10 limsup
comparison therefore holds against each. On the nested-before-second
pair class the stronger finite factor133613/134064<1 holds for m>=1024.
The precise finite complementary-pair bound is (D15).

Condition (D0) says the initial vertical coordinate is at or below the
highest minimum. It includes the ENTIRE originally assigned family and
enlarges the canonical residual range to -Mv<=beta_c<0, where beta_c is
the beta in (D5). This new legal range is nonempty, as checked below. It is
an explicit restriction, NOT a lossless reduction of arbitrary signed
costs. The complementary upper-projection branch H_c<0 remains OPEN
as a whole-family comparison. Section5 constructs legal ORIGINAL-word
data in that branch, prices them below complete references, and shows
that neither a zero minimum nor (D0) can be silently assumed.

## 1. Changed hypothesis, original rows and exact minimum geometry

Fix the original chronological word
h=(block_0,b,block_1,d,block_2). Its blocks retain every actual query,
including zero steps. Define S_j=sum block_j, T=1+S_0, M=1+S_1,
N=1+S_2, A=b-1>0, E=d-1>0, and sigma=2N-1.
Subscript o marks the original, untranslated parameters. Choose

    u>=0, v>0, w>0, ell>0,
    alpha_o>=0, beta_o>=0, kappa_o>=0, tau in R,
    p=(1,0), q=(-u,-v), r=(-w,ell),
    delta_o=A-Mu-alpha_o, H_o=Mv+beta_o,
    a_q=u^2+v^2, a_r=w^2+ell^2, c=<q,r>=uw-v ell.

The ONLY changed original hypothesis is the final-plane cost: tau was0
in the assigned family. Define the single static affine maximum and
its Moreau envelope by

    J_o(y)=max{0,<p,y>,<q,y>-kappa_o,<r,y>-tau},
    F_o(x)=min_y {J_o(y)+||x-y||^2/2},
    X_0=(T+delta_o,-H_o).                              (D1)

The minimum level is NOT assumed to be0. The following are the complete
endpoint tests for the specified p,q,r holding blocks. Put
z_o=(Eu-alpha_o,Ev-beta_o),
R_o=u alpha_o+v beta_o-kappa_o,
eta_o=w alpha_o-ell beta_o-tau, G_o=R_o-eta_o. Then require

    delta_o>=0,
    v H_o <= (1+u)delta_o+kappa_o,
    (1+w)delta_o+ell H_o+tau >=0;
    R_o>=0, G_o>=0, G_o+(M-1)(a_q-c)>=0;
    <r,z_o>-tau-N a_r>=0;
    <r-p,z_o>-tau-N(a_r+w)>=0;
    <r-q,z_o>+kappa_o-tau-(a_r-c)>=0,
    <r-q,z_o>+kappa_o-tau-N(a_r-c)>=0.                 (D2)

To prove necessity and sufficiency at every physical query, the proposed
proximal points are (delta_o+S_0-t,-H_o) in the first block,
(-alpha_o,-beta_o)+(S_1-t)q in the middle block, and z_o-k r in the
last block, where t is the actual cumulative mass and 1<=k<=N.
Each pair of affine heights is affine in t or k. In the first block,
p-minus-zero, p-minus-q and p-minus-r have positive loss rates1,1+u,
1+w; their final tests are the first line. In the middle block the
q-minus-zero margin is R_o+(S_1-t)a_q. The p height is nonpositive,
so its comparison follows. The q-minus-r margin is
G_o+(S_1-t)(a_q-c), which needs both endpoints. In the tail,
r-minus-zero and r-minus-p have positive rates a_r,a_r+w, while
r-minus-q has unrestricted rate a_r-c and needs both endpoints.
These arguments include each checkpoint query and the terminal sample.

An active plane supplies its slope as a subgradient; x=y+g then proves
that y is the proximal minimizer by the subgradient inequality and a
quadratic expansion. Conversely the subdifferential of a finite affine
maximum is the convex hull of its active slopes. To see the converse,
a vector outside this hull is separated by a direction, contradicting
the directional derivative, the maximum of active directional slopes.
The slopes p,q,r are uniquely exposed by (1,0),(0,-1),(0,1). Thus having
one of these slopes as a subgradient forces its own plane to be active.
This proves necessity as well as sufficiency of (D2).

Set

    D_min=ell(1+u)+v(1+w)>0,
    mu_0=-(ell kappa_o+v tau)/D_min, mu=max(0,mu_0),
    y_minus=[-kappa_o-(1+u)mu]/v,
    y_plus=[tau+(1+w)mu]/ell.                         (D3)

Then min J_o=min F_o=mu. If mu_0<=0, the minimum set is

    K_o={x_1<=0,
         (-kappa_o-u x_1)/v<=x_2<=(tau+w x_1)/ell}.

It is a triangle when ell kappa_o+v tau>0 and a singleton at equality.
Its right edge is {0} times [y_minus,y_plus]. If mu_0>0, the minimum
set is the singleton (mu,y_plus)=(mu,y_minus).

Proof of the level formula: the positive weights
(u ell+v w)/D_min, ell/D_min, v/D_min sum to1 and make the weighted
sum of p,q,r zero. The corresponding weighted affine heights equal
mu_0 at every point. Thus the three-plane maximum is at least mu_0.
All three heights equal mu_0 at
(mu_0,[tau+(1+w)mu_0]/ell), so that is its attained minimum. Equality
in a positive weighted average forces all three planes equal, yielding
uniqueness when mu_0>0. When mu_0<=0, the three zero sublevels intersect
exactly as in K_o; their right-edge interval is nonempty precisely
when ell kappa_o+v tau>=0. This proves all cases, including equality.

The envelope is finite, convex, differentiable and1-smooth. Indeed the
proximal objective is coercive and strictly convex. Its unique minimizer
y(x) satisfies x-y(x) in the subdifferential of J_o; comparison with
y(x)+t(z-y(x)) and t decreasing to0 proves this optimality condition.
The two subgradient inequalities give
||g(x)-g(x')||^2<=<g(x)-g(x'),x-x'> for g=x-y. Evaluating the proximal
objectives at the old minimizer and using the subgradient inequality
at the new minimizer bounds the first-order error between0 and
||x'-x||^2/2. Thus grad F_o=g and is1-Lipschitz. Convexity also follows
by taking convex combinations of proximal competitors. Since J_o>=mu,
an envelope value mu forces y=x and J_o(x)=mu. Conversely every minimum
of J_o gives envelope value mu. This proves equality of minimum sets.

For legal data delta_o>=mu, because the active p-plane has value
delta_o at its ending proximal point and J_o>=mu. Every minimum has
x_1<=mu and y_minus<=x_2<=y_plus; these coordinate bounds are attained
simultaneously on the displayed right edge, or at the singleton. Since
X_0,1=T+delta_o>mu, the exact closest point is therefore

    X_star=(mu, clip(-H_o,[y_minus,y_plus])),
    R^2=(T+delta_o-mu)^2
          +dist(-H_o,[y_minus,y_plus])^2.             (D4)

Here clip(t,[a,b])=min(b,max(a,t)) and dist is Euclidean distance.
The closest radius R in this document is not the project's alternate
ratio convention R in W=2R. No chosen but nonclosest minimizer is used.

## 2. Exact canonical reduction, optimum rows and paid continuation

Translate by c_min=(mu,y_plus) and subtract the actual minimum mu.
The resulting envelope is F(x)=F_o(x+c_min)-mu, with affine maximum

    J(y)=max{0,<p,y>,<q,y>-kappa,<r,y>},
    kappa=kappa_o+(1+u)mu+v y_plus
          =v(y_plus-y_minus)>=0,
    alpha=alpha_o+mu, beta=beta_o+y_plus,
    delta=delta_o-mu=A-Mu-alpha>=0,
    H=Mv+beta,
    X_0^c=(T+delta,-H).                              (D5)

If mu>0 the translated original zero plane is the constant -mu.
The positive barycentric relation with kappa=0 shows that the other
three translated planes have maximum at least0 everywhere. Thus
replacing -mu by0 changes no function. If mu=0 the zero plane stays0.
This proves the exact functional reduction, not just equality of jets.
The GD word and every physical query are unchanged by translation.

The new residual beta is allowed to be ANY real number that arises.
The canonical minimum is

    K={x_1<=0,(-kappa-u x_1)/v<=x_2<=(w/ell)x_1},
    R^2=(T+delta)^2+(H-kappa/v)_+^2+(-H)_+^2.          (D6)

The interval at x_1=0 is [-kappa/v,0]; its projection formula proves
(D6), including the lower, interior and upper vertical branches.
The originally assigned beta>=0 case has H>0 and no upper contribution.

In canonical variables put z=(Eu-alpha,Ev-beta),
R_q=u alpha+v beta-kappa, eta=w alpha-ell beta and G=R_q-eta.
All tests in (D2) hold with tau=0 and these canonical parameters; retain
the additional prefix condition (1+w)delta+ell H>=0 when H<0.
The proof of the middle p comparison uses alpha>=0, not beta>=0.
Thus it remains valid after the change of sign. Necessity and sufficiency
follow from the explicit proximal points already proved.

Assign costs0,0,kappa,0 to slopes0,p,q,r. At any physical sample,
f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2-k_i. For all ordered pairs, including remote
queries, the project row convention of accepted1252b8823bb5a290 gives

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j>-k_i+k_j<=0.               (D7)

This is precisely activity of j's plane against i's plane. Include the
actual closest X_star^c with slope, cost and value0: its zero plane is
active, so (D7) proves both optimum orientations as well. If its second
coordinate is y_star, the optimum slacks are0,kappa+v y_star,-ell y_star;
each is nonnegative because -kappa/v<=y_star<=0. Values in original
coordinates differ by mu, which cancels from every Q row. No optimum
row or physical query is deleted, even when steps repeat or vanish.

The exact normalized payoff and ending tail margins are

    V=2(F_o(X_n)-mu)/R^2=P/R^2,
    P=2eta-2E c-sigma a_r=2L_0+a_r>0,
    L_0=<r,z>-N a_r,
    L_p=<r-p,z>-N(a_r+w),
    L_q=<r-q,z>+kappa-N(a_r-c).                       (D8)

For any appended nonnegative list of total mass zeta, this SAME function
holds r at each appended query exactly while

    0<=zeta<=min{L_0/a_r,L_p/(a_r+w),
                  L_q/(a_r-c) if a_r>c, else +infinity}.
    V(zeta)=V(0)-2zeta a_r/R^2.                      (D9)

Each margin is its displayed initial value minus zeta times its loss
rate; affine dependence covers every physical appended query. A
nonpositive q loss rate gives no upper restriction. The exposedness of
r proves necessity. This charges all three continuation conditions.
For arbitrary L>0 use L F_o and steps h_i/L; the trajectory and V agree.

## 3. Closing the new negative-residual range H>=0

The beta>=0 range is proved jointly in Appendix J. We now cover EVERY
remaining tuple beta<0, H>=0, on EVERY original checkpoint pair. Put
b_neg=-beta>0. Then b_neg<=Mv. The retained middle row G>=0 gives

    0<eta=w alpha+ell b_neg
        <=u alpha-v b_neg-kappa<=u alpha.             (D10)

Consequently u alpha>0. Prefix q activity and R_q>=0 give

    Mv^2<=delta+u(A-Mu),
    (H-kappa/v)_+ >= (Mv-u alpha/v)_+.

The full-norm inequality (A15), proved completely in Appendix J, applies
for T>=2A and implies

    (u^2+v^2)/R^2<=C(T,A,M),
    C(T,A,M)=max{A^2/(M^2T^2),1/(T+A+M)^2}.           (D11)

There is no assumption beta>=0 in the hypotheses of that inequality.
Every original H_m pair with m>=128 has A/T<=rho=c_m-2<1/2, so its
word condition is satisfied. The same original-word derivation appears
in Appendix J and uses the enclosing-m parameters throughout.

Let y=(-alpha,b_neg) be the ending middle proximal point. Its q height
before cost subtraction is <q,y>=u alpha-v b_neg>=kappa+eta>0. Also

    ||y||^2=alpha^2+b_neg^2
        <=alpha^2+M v b_neg<=alpha^2+Mu alpha<=A^2.

The first inequality uses b_neg<=Mv, the second uses (D10), and the
last uses alpha+Mu=A-delta<=A and alpha<=A. Since z=y-Eq,
completing the terminal r square proves

    P<=||z||^2/sigma
      =[||y||^2+E^2 a_q-2E<q,y>]/sigma.

Therefore, with r_0=A/T,

    V<=[r_0^2+E^2 C(T,A,M)]/sigma.                    (D12)

Independently the exact terminal identity
P=E^2 a_q/sigma+2eta
  -sigma(ell-Ev/sigma)^2
  -(sqrt(sigma)w+Eu/sqrt(sigma))^2
and (D10) imply
2eta/R^2<=2u alpha/R^2<=A^2/(2M T^2)=r_0^2/(2M).
Combining these TWO valid bounds gives

    V<=E^2 C(T,A,M)/sigma
       +r_0^2 min{1/sigma,1/(2M)}.                   (D13)

This is a closed quantitative bound on the whole new range, not a free
optimization or a feasibility statement. No positive reserve is deleted.

For an explicit nonempty new range on every original pair, take the
notation Q,t,u,w of (D18), but set alpha=A-Mu, delta=kappa=0,
v^2=u alpha/[2M(1+t)], ell=t v and beta=-Mv. Then H=0 and beta<0.
The prefix p/q and p/r margins both equal0; at the middle end
R_q=eta=u alpha(1+2t)/[2(1+t)]>0 and G=0. The tail r/q formula and
strict r/zero and r/p checks in Section5 apply with this new v, since
alpha>=3A/4>u(E+N/2). Thus all physical rows hold. Translation (D20)
again gives original alpha_o,beta_o,kappa_o>=0 and a signed terminal
cost: its beta_o formula is unchanged because Mv^2=u alpha/[2(1+t)].
This state is in the added range and already priced by (D12)--(D15).

For pairs whose first checkpoint is inside the H_l directly preceding
the second, the entire word comparison of Appendix J Section6 uses
only (D12), the original adjacent-child masses and their fixed enclosing
parameters. It gives the finite factor133613/134064 for m>=1024.

For every complementary pair define Q=M+N+E and rho=c_m-2. The exact
original-word conditions are

    A<=rho min(T,Q), E<=rho min(M,N),
    sqrt2-1<=rho<=1/2, c_m=2+rho.                     (D14)

At fixed A,M,N,E, (2(T+A+Q)-1) times the right side of (D13) decreases
with T. For the C term this is proved by the positive denominators
T^2 and(T+A+M)^2: the derivative of (2T+C_0)/(T+D_0)^2 is negative
when C_0>2D_0, which holds for both terms. For the residual term,
write T=A/r_0. Its factor depending on r_0 is
2A r_0+[2(A+Q)-1]r_0^2, which increases for r_0>0.
Thus set T=A/rho in the scalar UPPER BOUND, without changing any word.

Define

    H_bar=rho/(2M)-sigma/(16M^2), if sigma<=4rho M;
          rho^2/sigma, otherwise.

The new residual rho^2 min{1/sigma,1/(2M)} is at most H_bar.
If sigma<=4rho M, then sigma<=2M and
rho^2/(2M)<=rho/(4M)<=H_bar, using rho<=1/2.
Otherwise H_bar=rho^2/sigma already dominates the minimum.

The pair term of C grows with A after the scalar prefix replacement,
so A<=rho Q bounds it at A=rho Q. For the other term put
B_loc=T+A+M in [M,M+(1+rho)Q]. Its normalized expression is
[2(B_loc+N+E)-1][H_bar+E^2/(sigma B_loc^2)]. It is convex in B_loc,
because its non-affine part is a positive multiple of
2/B_loc+(2N+2E-1)/B_loc^2. The endpoint at M and the endpoint at
M+(1+rho)Q give, respectively, the following finite bound:

    (2(T+A+Q)-1)V<=max{UB_0,UB_1},
    UB_0=(2Q-1)[H_bar+E^2/(sigma M^2)],
    UB_1=(2c_m Q-1)[H_bar+rho^2 E^2/(sigma M^2)].       (D15)

For the second endpoint use B_loc>=c_m M>=M/rho, since c_m rho>=1.
This rederives the complete finite word comparison for the new range.

The analytical limit of these very same UB_0,UB_1 is derived in full
in Appendix J C.4, with separate cases Q bounded, Q unbounded with
N/M tending to0, a finite positive value, or infinity. For Q bounded,
use (D12) directly as T tends to infinity. For Q unbounded the explicit
bound (D15) is identical to the one analyzed there. Its upper limit is
at most13/10 uniformly over ALL pairs and parameters; the proof uses
symbolic square completion and compact subsequence selection, not any
numerical evaluation. The nested class has its smaller finite constant.

Combining this new entire range with beta>=0 from Appendix J proves
the main claim for EVERY legal canonical tuple with H>=0. The exact
signed-cost transformation (D5) proves the stated original-coordinate
claim under (D0). Appendix J also proves complete F_ray=K=1/(2M_m-1)
on ORIGINAL H_m and limsup B_(2^m-1)/(2M_m-1)<=2/exp(1), using the
ORIGINAL all-split recurrence. These calculations are reproduced there
as part of this joint proof; none is imported from an unverified finding.

## 4. The remaining upper-projection branch: a paid necessary bound

Here H<0, put d_up=-H>0, so beta=-(Mv+d_up), and a_0=A-delta=Mu+alpha.
The actual radius is (T+delta)^2+d_up^2. In addition to ALL canonical
tests, retain

    ell d_up<=(1+w)delta,
    G=(u-w)alpha-(v+ell)(Mv+d_up)-kappa>=0.            (D16)

Both terms are actual support costs. Let k_0=2(1+E/M)>=2. Substituting
alpha=a_0-Mu into P from (D8) gives the exact identity

    P=k_0 u alpha-k_0 Mv^2-(k_0-2)a_0 w
       -k_0 v d_up-(k_0-2)ell d_up-sigma a_r
       -k_0 kappa-k_0 G.

Every subtracted term is nonnegative. In particular

    P+sigma a_r <= (M+E)(A-delta)^2/(2M^2),
    V <= (M+E)A^2/(2M^2T^2).                         (D17)

This is an actual upper bound with the middle intercept and physical
support margin paid. It does not by itself price every original pair,
especially a long tail or a large unselected mass outside the middle.
No assertion that its unknown optimal combination with the other rows
matches the original benchmark is made. The WHOLE H<0 signed-cost
route is BLOCKED at the complete original-pair comparison in this
allocation; a generic feasibility/profile reformulation is not progress.

## 5. Actual original-word counterexample to deleting the upper branch

Take the first and last level0 checkpoints of ORIGINAL H_m, m>=128.
Keep the SAME enclosing parameters: T=N=1, A=E=a_m<1/2 and
M=M_m-2-2A>=1, where M_m=1+sum H_m. More generally the following
formulas work under A/(M+E+N)<1/2. Put

    Q=M+E+N, t=E/(2N), u=A/(4Q), w=u/2,
    alpha=(A-Mu)/[1+t u/(4(1+t))],
    delta=t u alpha/[4(1+t)],
    v=sqrt(u alpha/[4M(1+t)]), ell=t v,
    beta=-2Mv, kappa=0.                              (D18)

All parameters used as magnitudes are positive. Because u<1/8,
Mu<=A/4 and the denominator defining alpha is less than3/2,
alpha>A/2. Thus u(E+N/2)<=A/4<alpha. The budget identity
delta+Mu+alpha=A holds. Here H=-Mv<0 and d_up=Mv>0.

Every support test is now checked explicitly. The prefix q inequality
holds since vH=-Mv^2<0. The p/r prefix margin is
(1+w)delta+ell H=w delta>0, because delta=t Mv^2.
At the middle end,

    R_q=eta=u alpha(1+2t)/[2(1+t)]>0, G=0,
    a_q-c=u^2/2+(1+t)v^2>0.

At ANY tail position 1<=k<=N its r/q margin is

    E(a_q-c)-k(a_r-c)
      =u^2(E/2+k/4)+(1+t)(E-kt)v^2>0,

because E-kt>=E/2. Its ending r/zero margin is

    L_0=eta-(u^2/2)(E+N/2)+t(E-Nt)v^2
       >=(u/2)[alpha-u(E+N/2)]>0.

At every tail proximal point its first coordinate is
Eu-alpha+k w<=u(E+N/2)-alpha<0. Thus the r/zero test also gives
r/p activity. Prefix p/zero and middle q/zero and q/p follow from
delta>0, R_q>0 and alpha>0. This proves ALL (D2) tests, both original
physical row orientations and all optimum rows via (D7). The closest
minimum is uniquely0, and

    R^2=(T+delta)^2+M^2v^2,
    P=2eta-2E(u^2/2-t v^2)-sigma(u^2/4+t^2v^2).       (D19)

The holding law is EXACTLY (D9), with all three displayed margins.
This is one static function throughout, not an iteratively reassigned
oracle or an unpriced gradient sequence.

It can be written inside the ORIGINAL nonnegative-residual signed-cost
family (D1), with POSITIVE actual minimum. Set

    mu_ex=alpha/2, y_ex=-(1+u)mu_ex/v,
    alpha_o=alpha-mu_ex=alpha/2,
    beta_o=beta-y_ex
          =alpha[1+u-u/(1+t)]/(2v)>0,
    kappa_o=0, delta_o=delta+mu_ex,
    tau=-mu_ex[(1+w)+t(1+u)]<0.                      (D20)

The original three nonzero planes tie at (mu_ex,y_ex) with value mu_ex.
Their positive barycentric identity gives J_o>=mu_ex everywhere;
the zero plane is redundant. Hence F_o(x)=mu_ex+F(x-(mu_ex,y_ex)).
This proves legality of the original parameters by the exact functional
translation, and its actual minimum is the singleton (mu_ex,y_ex).
Formula (D3) indeed returns mu=mu_ex and y_plus=y_ex. In particular
mu is not0 and H_c=-Mv<0. The denominator missing the upper term would
be (T+delta)^2, whereas the actual denominator is larger by M^2v^2>0.
This is a counterexample to deleting the upper projection branch, not
to the scoped bound proved under (D0).

Price this ACTUAL witness against COMPLETE same-word references.
For the selected level0 pair t=A/2<1/4. From (D19), after dropping
only negative terms,

    P<=2eta+2E t v^2
      <=(6/5+1/16)u alpha
      <=101 A^2/(320M).

The first coefficient uses (1+2t)/(1+t)<=6/5; the second uses
E t/[2M(1+t)]<=A^2/(4M)<=1/16. Also R^2>=1 and
2M_m-1=2M+3+4A<=7M. By the COMPLETE reference calculation in
Appendix J,

    V/K_(2^m-1)(H_m)=V/F_ray_(2^m-1)(H_m)
      <707/1280<1.                                   (D21)

Both full Huber-augmented complex/lens references are at least K, so
this comparison holds against them too. The ORIGINAL-B limsup is at
most707/[640 exp(1)]<1. Thus the legal example only establishes the
necessity of the changed minimum/radius geometry. It does not supply
an improving adversary or evidence that the full H<0 family is strong.

## 6. Counterexample search, exact scope and handoff

Textual attacks on the main extension included both minimum-level
branches mu_0<=0 and mu_0>0; the equality triangle-collapse case;
beta changing sign; H=0; kappa=0; both lower-radius branches; and the
case of a nonpositive final r/q loss rate. The explicit state (D18)--(D20)
refutes a lossless reduction to H>=0 and an assumed zero minimum.
It is legal on an actual original word and is priced by (D21).

No counterexample is claimed to (D10)--(D15): these inequalities have
the direct derivations included here. In particular, ||y||<=A used
b_neg<=Mv; applying it to all H<0 tuples would delete its hypothesis.
The new residual bound is the minimum of two independently proved
bounds, not the result of maximizing different terms at one legal tuple.
Replacing T or A in an UPPER BOUND never purports to change the word.
Neither mu nor the endpoint intercept is treated as a free value credit.

The completed deliverable is a WHOLE assigned-family obstruction and
its explicit larger signed-cost subfamily extension (D0), uniformly
over every original checkpoint pair. The entire unrestricted signed-cost
extension is not closed. Any new mechanism within this two-checkpoint
geometry must leave (D0), or change another named hypothesis such as
the gradient half-planes, the number of stages or variation inside a
holding block. The actual H<0 calibration has already been priced as
weak; do not cite its mere legality as benchmark progress.

The original all-n/all-h target and full-class upper alternative remain
OPEN. No claim of completion, publication, finalize, shutdown or new
formal allocation is made. This is the final originally allocated round;
no self-renewal is inferred from the open upper-projection question.

Literature process: search-math-results, active_program / mutation.
Matlas returned2303.08785 Lemma4.1 (Moreau regularity),1504.00424
Lemma2.2 (active-slope subdifferential), and an irrelevant polynomial
positivity theorem. The exact local Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur paper
1502.05666 was read at Section2.5, Theorem4 and its proof/construction,
and Corollary1. The paper's finite-dimensional smooth convex definition
fits our regime, but its result is not a proof dependency: all required
regularity and plane tests are derived here. Primary background source:
https://arxiv.org/abs/1502.05666. No new literature black box is used.

## Appendix J. Full joint core for the beta>=0 range and original-word pricing

The following mathematical core is reproduced in full from the earlier
own draft and has been reread in this round. It is part of THIS joint
unverified proof, not an invocation of a finding as an accepted fact.
Its historical allocation/status statements concern that source draft;
the present allocation and enlarged scope are those stated above.

# C105: charged obtuse endpoints and a whole original-word obstruction

Status: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT, allocation round36 of ceiling37.
This is not a fact. It closes the declared obtuse family at draft level,
including every original checkpoint pair, but not the original target.
The current assignment and guidance48a4276698805f28 permit textual work
and shared drafts only. No computation or formal verification is used.

Original target: with the original last-iterate, true nearest-minimizer
normalization W=2R, prove W_n^*/U_n^ZJ ->1 uniformly over all sufficiently
large lengths and all predetermined nonnegative words, or certify a fixed
relative asymptotic full-class upper improvement. All finite dimensions,
zero and unbounded steps, and one static function per run remain allowed.
Nothing below is an upper bound on the unrestricted W_n.

The accepted inputs are1252b8823bb5a290 (row convention and normalization),
b98bb807d514e80d (complete K and original benchmark definitions),
1fca3e5dd555cb4a (complete ray channel), and dfde2fa326c908bf (complex/lens
channel). C101--C104 are retrieval evidence, not correctness predecessors.
All new ingredients used below are derived within this joint document.

MAIN CONCLUSION. For the entire legal family (1)--(6), on every original
H_m checkpoint pair with enclosing-m parameters unchanged, let V_m^obt
be the supremum of the exact true-distance payoff. Then

    limsup_(m->infinity) V_m^obt/K_(2^m-1)(H_m)<=13/10,
    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) V_m^obt<=13/[5 exp(1)]<1. (0)

Here the COMPLETE same-word K equals COMPLETE F_ray and is dominated
by both full Huber-augmented complex/lens references. Section6 gives
the stronger finite factor133613/134064<1 for all parameters in one
structural pair class. Appendix C prices ALL remaining pairs, including
positive eta and small E, and proves the uniform asymptotic statement.
An obtuse instance might improve a reference by less than the bound;
we do not claim V_m^obt<=K. The whole family nevertheless misses the
original profile by a fixed relative asymptotic amount. This is an
adversary-family obstruction, not an upper bound on unrestricted W_n.

## 1. Exact declared family and every physical support test

Fix the ORIGINAL word h=(block_0,b,block_1,d,block_2). Put

    S_j=sum block_j, T=1+S_0, M=1+S_1, N=1+S_2,
    A=b-1>0, E=d-1>0, sigma=2N-1.

Blocks contain their actual chronological physical steps, including zeros.
These letters are local abbreviations, not the original B_n benchmark.
Choose independent u>=0,v>0,alpha>=0,beta>=0,kappa>=0,w>0,ell>0. Set

    delta=A-Mu-alpha, H=Mv+beta,
    p=(1,0), q=(-u,-v), r=(-w,ell),
    a_q=u^2+v^2, a_r=w^2+ell^2, c=<q,r>=uw-v ell,
    z=(Eu-alpha,Ev-beta), eta=w alpha-ell beta,
    R_q=u alpha+v beta-kappa, G=R_q-eta.

Here eta is the r-plane height at the ending middle proximal point;
it is not a free reserve. Define one static finite affine maximum

    J(y)=max{0,<p,y>,<q,y>-kappa,<r,y>},
    F(x)=min_y {J(y)+||x-y||^2/2}, X_0=(T+delta,-H).       (1)

The complete endpoint tests for the stated constant gradients p,q,r are

    delta>=0, vH<=(1+u)delta+kappa, R_q>=0;              (2)
    G>=0, G+(M-1)(a_q-c)>=0;                            (3)
    <r,z>-N a_r>=0;                                    (4)
    <r-q,z>+kappa-(a_r-c)>=0,
    <r-q,z>+kappa-N(a_r-c)>=0;                          (5)
    <r-p,z>-N(a_r+w)>=0.                               (6)

Unlike the positive-quadrant case, (3) is not automatic, and the rate
a_r-c in (5) is not presumed positive. Both actual endpoints are kept.
The prefix r comparison is automatic: at its proximal point
y=(delta+S_0-t,-H), the p-minus-r margin is
(1+w)(delta+S_0-t)+ell H>0 for every physical t in[0,S_0].
The p-versus-zero and p-versus-q margins are minimized at t=S_0;
their values are exactly the first two conditions in (2).

Checkpoint b sends the iterate to(-Mu-alpha,-Mv-beta). At middle
cumulative mass t in[0,S_1] its proposed proximal point is

    y=(-alpha,-beta)+(S_1-t)q.

The q-versus-zero margin is R_q+(S_1-t)a_q>=0. Its margin over p
is nonnegative because p's height is nonpositive. Its margin over r
is G+(S_1-t)(a_q-c), so (3) proves every physical middle query.
The second checkpoint sends the iterate to z. At tail cumulative
mass t in[0,S_2], put k=1+t, so y=z-k r with1<=k<=N. The r-minus-zero
margin has positive loss rate a_r; the r-minus-p margin has positive
loss rate a_r+w. Their final tests are (4),(6). The r-minus-q margin
has rate a_r-c of unrestricted sign, so (5) proves every physical tail
query. This includes the actual terminal point.

These tests are necessary as well as sufficient for the specified
gradients of (1). A subgradient of a finite affine maximum is a convex
combination of its active slopes: otherwise a separating direction
contradicts its directional derivative, the maximum active directional
slope. The slopes p,q,r are uniquely exposed by directions(1,0),(0,-1),
(0,1), respectively. Hence their occurrence as subgradients requires
their respective planes to be active. Evaluating activity at the stated
endpoints gives exactly (2)--(6).

## 2. Static regularity and the actual nearest minimizer

J is continuous, finite, convex and nonnegative. Its proximal objective
is coercive and strictly convex, hence has a unique minimizer y(x).
Comparison with y+t(v-y), convexity and t decreasing to0 show
g(x)=x-y(x) belongs to the subdifferential of J at y(x). Conversely,
that subgradient inequality and the quadratic expansion prove proximal
optimality. Adding the subgradient inequalities at two points gives
||g(x)-g(x')||^2<=<g(x)-g(x'),x-x'>. In particular g is1-Lipschitz.
Evaluating the proximal objective at the old minimizer gives the upper
first-order bound for F. Applying its subgradient inequality at the new
minimizer and completing the quadratic gives the lower bound. Thus

    0<=F(x')-F(x)-<g(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2/2.

This proves that F is convex, differentiable and1-smooth with gradient g.
Its minimum is0 and its minimum set is exactly

    K={x_1<=0, -w x_1+ell x_2<=0, -u x_1-v x_2<=kappa}. (7)

Indeed J=0 gives F=0, and an attained zero proximal objective forces
both nonnegative terms to vanish. The geometry in (7) has been rederived
for w>0: it lies below the line x_2=(w/ell)x_1<=0 and above
x_2=(-kappa-u x_1)/v>=-kappa/v. It is a triangle when kappa>0 and
the singleton0 when kappa=0. Its left vertex has
x_1=-kappa/(u+vw/ell); the denominator is positive even when u=0.

Since T+delta>0 and H>0, the closest point to X_0 is

    t_star=min(H,kappa/v), x_star=(0,-t_star),
    D=(H-kappa/v)_+,
    R^2=dist(X_0,K)^2=(T+delta)^2+D^2.                  (8)

Proof: every point of K has first coordinate<=0 and second coordinate
>=-kappa/v. These separate lower bounds on squared coordinate distances
are simultaneously attained at the displayed point, which satisfies all
three inequalities of (7). This includes both positive-part branches.
Here R denotes this local true distance, not the project's ratio in W=2R.

## 3. All directed rows, optimum slacks, payoff and holding price

Assign slopes0,p,q,r affine costs0,0,kappa,0, respectively. At any of
the physical samples, activity at y_i=x_i-g_i gives

    f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2-k_i,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<g_i-g_j,y_j>-k_i+k_j<=0.                     (9)

The last inequality is precisely j-plane activity against i's plane.
Thus every orientation, all remote physical pairs, and repeated/zero-step
queries satisfy the project convention of accepted1252b8823bb5a290.
Include x_star with slope, cost and value0. Its zero plane is active,
so (9) also proves both optimum orientations. No interpolation row is
deleted and no Gram-only realization is substituted for the function.

The ending-query affine reserves are delta,R_q,L_0, with

    L_0=<r,z>-N a_r,
    L_p=<r-p,z>-N(a_r+w),
    L_q=<r-q,z>+kappa-N(a_r-c).

The optimum slacks k_i-<g_i,x_star> are0,kappa-v t_star,ell t_star.
They are nonnegative and are distinct from the affine reserves.
The exact true-distance payoff is

    V=2F(X_n)/R^2=P/R^2,
    P=2<r,z>-sigma a_r
     =2eta-2E c-sigma a_r>0.                           (10)

Its positivity follows from (4): P=2L_0+a_r>=a_r>0.
Replacing F by L F for arbitrary L>0 keeps the same trajectory under
the original steps h_i/L and the same normalized payoff.

For an appended nonnegative list of total mass tau, the SAME function
holds gradient r at every appended physical query exactly while

    0<=tau<=min{L_0/a_r, L_p/(a_r+w),
               L_q/(a_r-c) if a_r>c, otherwise +infinity}. (11)

The three margins become L_0-tau a_r, L_p-tau(a_r+w),
L_q-tau(a_r-c). A nonpositive loss rate imposes no upper limit.
Exposedness of r supplies necessity. During this exact interval
V(tau)=V(0)-2tau a_r/R^2. No reserve by itself licenses continuation.

## 4. Positive residual is paid twice; a conditional complete comparison

Put eta_+=max(eta,0). From R_q>=0 and G>=0 one has

    kappa<=u alpha+v beta-eta_+.

Combining this with prefix condition (2) gives

    Mv^2+eta_+<=delta+u(A-Mu),
    D>=(Mv-u alpha/v+eta_+/v)_+.                        (12)

The first inequality charges the residual to prefix activity; the second
charges it to the true radius. Define only for an UPPER RELAXATION

    v_hat=sqrt(v^2+eta_+/M).

Then M v_hat^2<=delta+u(A-Mu), and

    R^2>=(T+delta)^2+(M v_hat-u alpha/v_hat)_+^2.        (13)

For (13), the positive-part numerator M v_hat^2-u alpha is the same
on both sides; its actual denominator v is no larger than v_hat.
This does not assert that changing v yields a legal new trajectory.

For T>=2A, the full-norm calculation proved in Appendix A gives

    (u^2+v_hat^2)/R^2<=C(T,A,M),
    C(T,A,M)=max{A^2/(M^2T^2),1/(T+A+M)^2}.             (14)

Completing the terminal squares, with their actual signs retained,

    P=E^2(u^2+v^2)/sigma+2eta
      -sigma(ell-Ev/sigma)^2
      -(sqrt(sigma)w+Eu/sqrt(sigma))^2.                 (15)

Therefore the ENTIRE legal obtuse parameter family on a word satisfying
T>=2A obeys

    V<=max{E^2/sigma,2M} C(T,A,M).                      (16)

More sharply, if eta<=0 OR E^2>=2M sigma, then

    V<= E^2 C(T,A,M)/sigma.                            (17)

In the second case,2eta_+<=E^2 eta_+/(M sigma); in the first case
the extra term is nonpositive. These are explicit domain restrictions,
not assertions about all obtuse endpoints. Appendix B proves directly
that on every ORIGINAL H_m pair,m>=128, the right side of (17) is at
most(61473/68840)K_n(H_m), with complete K=F_ray and the original-B
limsup at most2(61473/68840)/exp(1)<1. Thus (17) closes a substantial
declared subdomain, but (16) alone does not close its complement.

A second upper bound, valid for EVERY legal obtuse tuple when T>=2A,
is obtained by dropping the negative terms -2Euw,-2ell beta and
completing w and ell separately:

    V<= [A^2/T^2+E^2 C(T,A,M)]/sigma.                   (18)

Here alpha<=A and R>=T, and (14) with eta_+=0 follows from the weaker
conditions kappa<=u alpha+v beta. Again this is an upper relaxation.

## 5. Middle-to-terminal transport: the third price cannot be removed

Evaluate the retained q-plane margin at the actual terminal point:

    L_q=E(a_q-c)-N(a_r-c)-G>=0.

Together with G>=0 this gives the necessary inequality

    N a_r<=(E) a_q+(N-E)c.                             (19)

When e=E/N<=1, Cauchy--Schwarz implies ||r||<=||q||: setting
t=||r||/||q||>0 in (19) gives t^2<=(1-e)t+e, or
(t-1)(t+e)<=0. More specifically, direct coordinate factorization gives

    (w-u)(w+e u)+(ell+v)(ell-e v)<=0.                 (20)

If w>u, it follows that ell<e v and

    (w-u)(w+e u)<=(ell+v)(e v-ell)<=e v^2,             (21)

the last inequality using e<=1. These are necessary whole-function
conditions from BOTH directions of the physical q,r rows. Keeping only
the middle row permits false states and misses (19).

Here is a quantitative reserve bound using this transport. Suppose

    A/T<=rho, E/N<=rho, 0<rho<=1/2, eta>0.

Then every legal tuple satisfies

    2eta/R^2<=rho/(2M),
    2eta/R^2-sigma w^2/R^2
       <=max_(0<=x<=rho/(4M)){2x-sigma x^2/rho^2}.      (22)

Proof of the first inequality. If eta<=u alpha, then
2M eta<=2(Mu)alpha<=A^2/2, so2M eta/R^2<=rho^2/2<=rho/2.
If eta>u alpha, alpha>0. Write zeta=eta-u alpha>0 and set
b_0=zeta/v, x_0=Mv, B_0=Mu. From eta<=w alpha, one has
w-u>=zeta/alpha. By (21), b_0<=alpha sqrt(e)<=alpha sqrt(rho).
Formula (12) gives R^2>=(T+delta)^2+(x_0+b_0)^2>=T^2+(x_0+b_0)^2,
while2M eta=2alpha B_0+2x_0 b_0 and B_0<=A-alpha.
Let C_0=rho/2. Completing the square in x_0 proves

    2x_0 b_0-C_0(x_0+b_0)^2<=b_0^2(1/C_0-2).

Because1/C_0-2>=0, the right side is at most
alpha^2 rho(2/rho-2)=2(1-rho)alpha^2. Thus

    2M eta-C_0 R^2
       <=2A alpha-2rho alpha^2-C_0 T^2
       <=2(1-rho)A^2-(rho/2)T^2<=0.

The middle inequality uses alpha<=A and rho<=1/2, so the displayed
quadratic is increasing on[0,A]. The last follows from A<=rho T and
4rho(1-rho)<=1. This proves the first part of (22). For the second,
set x=eta/R^2. Since eta<=w alpha and alpha/R<=A/T<=rho,
w^2/R^2>=x^2/rho^2. The allowed interval for x is the first part.
The maximum is explicit:

    rho/(2M)-sigma/(16M^2), if sigma<=4rho M;
    rho^2/sigma,             if sigma>=4rho M.          (23)

Combining (15),(14),(23) yields an additional whole-family upper bound

    V<=E^2 C(T,A,M)/sigma + the quantity in (23).        (24)

For eta<=0 the same bound follows from (15) by dropping its nonpositive
residual term; thus (24) covers that case as well.

The original ruler has A/T<=rho=c_m-2 and E/N<=rho<1/2.
Appendix C sharpens (22) to retain A/T before taking the original-pair
supremum. This additional dependence is essential when a large part of
the word lies before the first checkpoint. Formula (24) alone is not
asserted to give a uniform whole-pair comparison.

## 6. Whole parameter comparison for one structural class of ORIGINAL pairs

Fix original enclosing m>=128, put lambda=1+sqrt2, and use beta_m=1-1/m,
c_m=(1+sqrt2)(1+1/m), a_m=beta_m/c_m, and original recursively nested
words H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_m c_m^j,H_j). Every child keeps these
SAME enclosing-m parameters. Write M_j=1+sum H_j and rho=c_m-2.

Consider ALL pairs whose first selected checkpoint lies inside the
particular H_l immediately preceding their second level-l checkpoint.
This entails l>=1. Let x be its local prefix mass plus1 in that H_l.
The original middle mass M and first excess A satisfy

    x+M+A=M_l=:V_l, T>=x, N>=V_l,
    A<=rho min(x,M), E<=rho V_l.                       (25)

The side bounds follow from the original adjacent H_k copies around
the first checkpoint, with a_k<=rho M_k as proved in Appendix B.
The full original mass is S=T+A+M+E+N. For either summand in (18),
its product with(2S-1) decreases as T or N increases while the other
listed quantities are fixed. For N this is the decreasing ratio
(2S-1)/(2N-1). For T each term is a positive constant times
(2T+C)/(T+D)^2 with C>2D, whose derivative is negative for T>=0.
For the first term D=0; for the second D=A+M and
C=2(A+M+E+N)-1>2D. For the pair term D=0 also.

We may therefore evaluate the upper bound at T=x,N=V_l. There
S=2V_l+E=M_(l+1). Put r_0=A/x and z_0=M/x. By (25),
r_0<=rho min(1,z_0), E/M<=rho[1+(1+r_0)/z_0]. If z_0>=1,
r_0<=rho and E/M<=rho(2+rho). If z_0<=1, then
r_0<=rho z_0 and
r_0 E/M<=rho^2[1+(1+rho)z_0]<=rho^2(2+rho).
In both cases

    (A/x)^2+(AE/(xM))^2<=rho^2+[c_m rho^2]^2.

The other branch in (18) has
(A/x)^2+(E/V_l)^2<=2rho^2<=rho^2+[c_m rho^2]^2,
because c_m rho>1: c_m>1+sqrt2 and that root obeys c_m(c_m-2)=1
at equality. Thus ALL parameters in this structural pair class obey

    (2S-1)V <= [rho^2+(c_m rho^2)^2]
               (2M_(l+1)-1)/(2M_l-1).                (26)

For m>=128, Appendix B gives rho<=9/20,c_m<=49/20,c_m rho^2<1/2.
Also a_m>2/5: using sqrt2<10/7 gives
a_m> (127/129)(7/17)=889/2193>2/5. Hence M_l>=M_1=2+a_m>12/5.
As M_(l+1)<=c_m M_l,

    (2M_(l+1)-1)/(2M_l-1)
       <=c_m+(c_m-1)/(2M_l-1)<=269/95.

Consequently the ENTIRE obtuse family over ALL pairs described by (25)
satisfies

    V<=(48689/38000) K_n(H_m), m>=128,                 (27)
    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) sup V
       <=2(48689/38000)/exp(1)<1.

Here48689/38000<4/3 and exp(1)>8/3 (its first five power-series terms
sum to65/24). Thus (27) is already a genuine original-target obstruction
for this whole pair class, even though its finite factor exceeds1.
It is not a claim that a tuple attaining that factor exists.

For a strict SAME-word reference comparison, take m>=1024. The rational
bound sqrt2<99/70 gives
c_m<(169/70)(1025/1024)<29/12, so rho<5/12 and
c_m rho^2<725/1728<3/7. The preceding mass ratio is at most53/19.
Equation (26) therefore gives

    V<=gamma_nested K_n(H_m)=gamma_nested F_ray_n(H_m),
    gamma_nested=(25/144+9/49)(53/19)
                 =133613/134064<1.                    (28)

The full Huber-augmented lens reference also dominates this SAME K value;
Appendix B retains its distinction from the full word-dependent complex
legal set. The maximum of the Huber value and the FULL word-dependent
complex channel also dominates the lens maximum, since Lens_n is a
subset of D_h by accepted dfde2fa326c908bf. No inequality against the
unaugmented complex component alone is claimed. No child word has been
assigned different parameters.

This closes EVERY obtuse parameter tuple on the nested-before-second
pair class. Appendix C treats every pair whose first checkpoint is
outside that particular preceding H_l, covering both the reverse-nested
case and a higher checkpoint strictly between the selected indices.

## 7. A new legal positive-residual state, not a benchmark gain

On any physical two-checkpoint word choose k=E/(2N) and

    theta=min{A/4, A^2/[32N(1+k)^2],
                   A^2 E/[32NM(1+k)]}>0,
    u=kappa=0, delta=2theta, alpha=A-2theta,
    v=sqrt(theta/M), beta=Mv,
    ell=k v, w=(1+k)theta/alpha.

Then alpha>=A/2. Prefix activity holds with vH=2theta=delta.
At the middle end, R_q=theta and eta=theta, so G=0, while
a_q-c=(1+k)v^2>0. The terminal q margin is

    L_q=(1+k)(E-Nk)v^2-Nw^2
       =(1+k)E theta/(2M)-Nw^2>0,

since Nw^2<=4N(1+k)^2theta^2/A^2 is at most
(1+k)E theta/(8M). The same estimate also gives Nw^2<=theta/8.
Thus L_0=theta+k(E-Nk)v^2-Nw^2>=7theta/8>0.
Moreover Nw/alpha<=1/[8(1+k)]<1, so the final proximal first
coordinate -alpha+Nw is negative; its p-plane is negative and (6)
holds. All tail q margins hold because a_r-c>0 and its final one
is positive. These explicit checks invoke (9) for every directed row.
The minimizer is uniquely0 and

    R^2=(T+2theta)^2+4Mtheta,
    P=2theta+theta E^2(2N+1)/(4MN^2)
                 -sigma(1+k)^2theta^2/(A-2theta)^2.    (29)

Both the middle reserve and positive contribution eta are theta;
neither is deleted. Every exact holding cost remains (11).

For the actual first and last level-0 checkpoints of ORIGINAL H_m,
T=N=1,A=E=a_m<1/2 and M=M_m-2-2A. The minimum defining theta is
its third entry: theta=A^3/[32M(1+A/2)]. This follows from M>=1,
A<1/2 by comparing that expression separately with the other two.
By (29), P<=(35/16)theta and R^2>=1. Since
2M_m-1=2M+3+4A<=2M+5<=7M,

    V/K_n(H_m)<=(245/512) A^3/(1+A/2)<245/4096<1.     (30)

This is a genuine positive-residual static law in the
complement of condition (17), eta>0,E^2<2M sigma, with every physical query paid. Its
complete SAME-word pricing is weak. It is not an improving adversary.

## 8. Counterexample screening and the original-target gap

The old sign-deletion shortcut is not reused: (15) retains2eta.
The new state in Section7 has eta>0 and positive middle reserve, so
the restricted eta<=0 case cannot be silently treated as exhaustive.
There is no counterexample claimed against the proved inequalities
(12),(19),(22),(26); these have direct proofs above, not a search verdict.

A tempting relaxation using the middle row alone allows positive eta
without the transport cost (19). Testing u=0, a middle q/r tie,
v^2=eta/M, beta=Mv, ell=k v and w=(1+k)eta/alpha exposes its failure:
the actual terminal q row demands

    N(1+k)^2 eta^2/alpha^2
       <=(1+k)(E-Nk)eta/M.

In particular k=1 is impossible when E<N. This diagnoses an ILLEGAL
relaxation; it is not an actual-word counterexample to any legal-family
upper bound. Section7 instead enforces this exact row and prices the
result against complete references. No numerical counterexample search
or machine algebra has been performed.

Appendix C completes the quantitative comparison in the domain
eta>0,E^2<2M(2N-1), including every first checkpoint outside the H_l
preceding the second. It gives an explicit finite upper bound and a
uniform analytical asymptotic price, rather than leaving a feasibility
profile as an unexplained technical lemma.

The original target remains OPEN. This obstruction depends on exactly
three gradient-holding blocks, p=(1,0), q=(-u,-v) with u>=0,v>0,
r=(-w,ell) with w,ell>0, independent nonnegative alpha,beta, and affine
costs0,kappa,0 with kappa>=0. A next mechanism must change at least one
of these hypotheses: for example first/final relative affine costs,
the terminal half-plane, the middle sign pattern, or the number of
gradient changes/variation within ordinary physical blocks. C99/C100
are not imported as unrestricted exclusions. No full-class upper
certificate or uniform arbitrary-word lower bound is produced here.

Additional symbolic boundary attacks on the complete comparison were
performed after Appendix C was assembled. At r_0^2=e_0/(1+2e_0), both
branches of C_res equal r_0^2 and D_C=1; no branch jump occurs. At
r_0=e_0=1/2, C_res=1/4, and with M=N=1 the exact residual bound is
3/16, agreeing with (C7). In the limit e_0 decreasing to0 with r_0 fixed,
C_res tends to r_0^2/2, so the same-side residual case is retained.
The D=0 radius branch can occur only in the eta<=u alpha part of the
reserve proof, since eta>u alpha would make its displayed vertical
radius lower bound strictly positive. For Q bounded and T unbounded,
the direct bound (18) tends to0 after original normalization; using only
the coarser C9 after reducing the prefix would miss this limiting case.
For Q unbounded, both N/M endpoints and all finite positive limits are
covered in C.4. These are author consistency checks with no independent
verification; no legal counterexample to the stated bounds was found.

## Appendix A. Joint proof of the full-norm inequality

The following calculation is included in full and rechecked for the
relaxed variables used in Section4. It is not an invocation of C104 as a fact.
Equation labels in this appendix carry the prefix A.


We prove from scratch: for A,M,T>0 with T>=2A, u>=0,v>0,alpha>=0,

    delta=A-Mu-alpha>=0,
    Mv^2<=delta+u(A-Mu),

one has

    (u^2+v^2)/[(T+delta)^2+(Mv-u alpha/v)_+^2]
       <=max{A^2/(M^2T^2),1/(T+A+M)^2}.                 (A15)

This controls the horizontal contribution that the former orthogonal
argument did not need. It includes both nearest-minimizer branches.

First suppose M<=A+2A^2/T. Put P=A-Mu and d=delta, so0<=d<=P.
The constraint gives M(u^2+v^2)<=Au+d. Direct expansion yields

    A^2(T+d)^2-M T^2(Au+d)
       =A P T^2+(2A^2T-MT^2)d+A^2d^2>=0.              (A16)

If the coefficient of d is nonnegative, every term is nonnegative.
If it is negative, substitute d<=P in just that term; the result is
at least P[(A-M)T^2+2A^2T]+A^2d^2>=0 by this regime's bound on M.
Dropping the second nonnegative radius square now proves the first
alternative A^2/(M^2T^2) in (A15). No saturation or endpoint limit is
needed in this regime.

Suppose next M>A+2A^2/T. Then M>A. For fixed u,alpha put

    P=A-Mu, d=P-alpha, k=1+u, c=uP,
    x=v^2, c_0=u alpha/M, H_0=T+d.

Legal v>0 forces P>0. The ratio on the left of (A15) is

    (u^2+x)/[H_0^2+M^2(x-c_0)_+^2/x].                  (A17)

On x<=c_0 it increases. On x>=c_0, differentiation has the sign of

    H_0^2-2M^2c_0-M^2u^2
      +2M^2c_0^2/x+M^2u^2c_0^2/x^2.

The first three terms are at least

    T^2-2Mu(A-Mu)-M^2u^2
       =T^2-A^2+(A-Mu)^2>=0.

The branches agree at x=c_0. Therefore (A17) is bounded by its value
at the allowed endpoint x=(d+c)/M. There
Mx-u alpha=(1+u)d>=0, giving the bound

    F_u(d)=(Au+d)/
       { M[(T+d)^2+M(1+u)^2d^2/(d+c)] }, 0<=d<=P.     (A18)

This saturation is only an upper relaxation; no final activity is assumed.

Write b_0=Au. For u>0, c>0, differentiation of (A18) has the sign of

    G_u(d)=(T+d)(T-d-2b_0)-M(1+u)^2 H_u(d),
    H_u(d)=d[(b_0+c)d+2b_0c]/(d+c)^2.

Explicitly

    H_u'(d)=2c^2(d+b_0)/(d+c)^3>=0,
    G_u'(d)=-2(d+b_0)-M(1+u)^2H_u'(d)<=0,
    G_u(P)=(T+A)(T-A-2Au).                            (A19)

The last identity follows by substituting P=A-Mu and d+c=P(1+u):
the subtracted term at P is Mu(A+P+2Au), which combined with the
first product leaves exactly the displayed factorization.

In the present regime

    u<=A/M<T/(T+2A)<=(T-A)/(2A).

The last inequality is equivalent to (T-2A)(T+A)>=0. Hence
G_u(P)>=0. Because G_u is nonincreasing, G_u(d)>=G_u(P)>=0 throughout
[0,P]; thus F_u(d) is nondecreasing. When u=0, its formula reduces to
d/{M[(T+d)^2+Md]}, whose derivative has the sign T^2-d^2>0 for
0<d<=A. Its limit at d=0 is0. These observations also handle all
relaxed endpoint degeneracies; the actual constraint always has v>0.

It follows that (A18) is at most its alpha=0 value at d=P. Writing
t=P in[0,A], that value is

    [A^2+(M-A)t]/[M^2(T^2+(2T+A+M)t)].                 (A20)

This is a quotient of positive affine functions of t. Its derivative
has the sign of

    (T+A)[M(T-A)-A(T+A)].

If nonpositive, the maximum is its value at t=0, namely A^2/(M^2T^2).
If nonnegative, the maximum is at t=A and equals
A/{M[(T+A)^2+MA]}. In the latter case

    M[(T+A)^2+MA]-A(T+A+M)^2
       =(T+A)[M(T-A)-A(T+A)]>=0,

so the value at t=A is at most1/(T+A+M)^2. This proves (A15).
Every relaxation, both radius branches, and the u=0 and boundary cases
have been retained. This is exactly the full-norm inequality required in Section4 of this joint draft.


## Appendix B. Joint original-word and reference comparison

All recurrences below use the enclosing-m parameters and every physical
index. The estimates are derived here; no prior draft is a premise.
Equation labels in this appendix carry the prefix B.


Fix the ORIGINAL enclosing m>=128, abbreviate a=a_m, c_r=c_m and
rho=c_r-2, and put a_k=a c_r^k for the level-k checkpoint excess.
The exact mass recurrence and its solution are

    M_0=1, M_(k+1)=2M_k+a c_r^k,
    M_k=[a/(c_r-2)]c_r^k+[1-a/(c_r-2)]2^k.              (B21)

The inequalities sqrt(2)<10/7 and m>=128 give

    12/5<lambda<c_r<49/20,
    0<a<lambda-2<rho<=9/20,
    M_k<=c_r^k, M_(k+1)<=c_r M_k, a_k<=rho M_k.         (B22)

For the upper bound on c_r, use
c_r<(17/7)(129/128)=2193/896<49/20. For the bound on a, use
a=(m-1)/[(m+1)lambda]<1/lambda=lambda-2. The remaining bounds follow
from the positive two-term formula (B21). In particular the term
(a/rho)c_r^k alone proves a_k<=rho M_k.

A level-k checkpoint has an immediately adjacent H_k before and after
it. Its complete prefix and suffix masses plus1 are therefore at least
M_k. For every first checkpoint in any pair, A/T<=rho<=9/20<1/2.
Thus T>=2A, as required by the new full-norm comparison.

For actual checkpoint indices i<j of levels k,l, respectively, put

    R_pair=AE/(TM), q_pair=c_r rho^2.

If i precedes the entire H_l immediately before j, then T>=M_k and
M>=M_l, so R_pair<=rho^2. This includes k>=l because such a level
cannot occur inside H_l. Otherwise i lies inside that particular H_l,
so k<l. Let x be1 plus i's local prefix mass inside H_l, and y=M.
Its local adjacent copies imply x,y>=M_k, and the exact identity is
x+y=M_l-a_k. As T>=x,

    TM>=xy>=M_k(M_l-a_k-M_k).

Write theta=a_k/M_k<=rho and zeta=M_l/M_k>=2+theta. The latter bound
follows from l>=k+1 and M_(k+1)=2M_k+a_k. Since E<=rho M_l,

    R_pair<=theta rho zeta/(zeta-1-theta)
           <=theta rho(2+theta)<=rho^2(2+rho)=q_pair.

The middle function decreases in zeta for zeta>=2+theta, and the
last expression increases in theta>=0. This covers every actual pair.
In particular q_pair<=(49/20)(9/20)^2=3969/8000<1/2.

Palindromicity applies this very same pair estimate in reverse and
gives AE/(NM)<=q_pair. The local side bounds give A<=rho T and
E<=rho N. Consequently

    R_pair<=q_pair,
    T<=q_pair N/R_pair,
    M<=rho^2 N/R_pair.

All quantities divided by here are positive. The full physical word
mass, including both checkpoints and every ordinary query, is exactly

    M_m=T+M+N+A+E.

Multiplying by R_pair and using the preceding bounds yields

    R_pair<=min{q_pair,D_pair N/M_m},
    D_pair=2(1+rho)q_pair+rho^2.                         (B23)

The constants satisfy

    q_pair D_pair<=2(29/20)(1/2)^2+(81/400)(1/2)
                  =661/800,
    D_pair/q_pair=2(1+rho)+1/c_r<=199/60<4.

The actual M_m is at least16: its word has2^m-1 steps, each greater
than1. Therefore N_0=q_pair M_m/D_pair>1. For1<=N<=N_0 use (B23)
and the increasing function N^2/(2N-1); for N>=N_0 use the other
branch and the decreasing function1/(2N-1). At their common crossover,

    R_pair^2/(2N-1)<=q_pair D_pair/(2M_m-D_pair/q_pair).

Relative to the SAME-word canonical Huber value, this gives

    [R_pair^2/(2N-1)]/[1/(2M_m-1)]
       <=(661/800)(2M_m-1)/(2M_m-199/60)
       <=(661/800)*31/(32-199/60)=61473/68840.           (B24)

The rational function decreases with M_m, so the final bound follows
from M_m>=16. The original tail factor2N-1 was retained throughout.

### B.2. Complete SAME-word K, ray and applicable lens references

For accepted b98bb807d514e80d, EVERY single-cut term at a level-k step
h=1+a_k has denominator

    (1+P)^2[1+2(S_total-h-P)], S_total=M_m-1,

where P is its COMPLETE physical prefix sum. Differentiation has the
sign of2(S_total-h)-3P, so its minimum between first and last occurrence
is at an endpoint. The first occurrence has prefix M_k-1 and suffix
at least M_(m-1)-1; for the last these are reversed. The first endpoint
is bounded using a_k<=rho M_k. For the other, use that x^2/(2x-1)
increases for x>=1 and M_k<=M_(m-1). Thus every cut is at most
rho^2/(2M_(m-1)-1). Since M_m<=c_r M_(m-1),

    Cut_i/[1/(2M_m-1)]<=rho^2(2c_r-1)
                         <=3159/4000<gamma104.          (B25)

Here (2c_r x-1)/(2x-1)<=2c_r-1 for x>=1. Hence the COMPLETE maximum
K_n(H_m) is its canonical Huber term1/(2M_m-1), not a sampled cut value.

For the complete F_ray_n reference use precisely the accepted recurrence
and feasibility conditions of1fca3e5dd555cb4a. If a checkpoint selection
has at least two indices, its first two nonterminal magnitudes are
m_0=1/T and m_1=A/(TM), and its old offset after the first cut is
O_1=(T-1)m_0. Its budget just after the second cut is

    E m_1-O_1=m_0[R_pair T-(T-1)]<m_0,

because R_pair<=q_pair<1. With exactly two cuts, feasibility requires
this budget at least N m_0>=m_0. With more cuts, the next nonterminal
magnitude is this budget divided by a mass at least1. A nonpositive
budget already violates positivity; a positive budget still gives
m_2<m_0, violating the required repeated-ray magnitude condition.
Thus no selection with two or more checkpoints is feasible. Zero and
single checkpoint choices are exactly the complete K terms. We obtain

    F_ray_n(H_m)=K_n(H_m)=1/(2M_m-1).                  (B26)

The second term of E^2 C(T,A,M)/sigma is the second actual checkpoint's single-cut
term, because its full prefix mass plus1 is T+A+M and suffix factor
is2N-1. Apply (B25) to it and (B24) to the pair term. Thus E^2 C(T,A,M)/sigma
is at most gamma104 K_n(H_m) on every original partition, with
gamma104=61473/68840. Section4 applies this ONLY in its stated domain.

The complete applicable reference from accepted dfde2fa326c908bf is

    V_lens(h)=max{sup_(z in Lens_n)Gap_h(z),1/(1+2sum h)}.

It contains this SAME Huber term by definition. Thus the upper bound
E^2 C(T,A,M)/sigma is at most gamma104 V_lens(H_m). This compares the
full Huber-augmented lens maximum without pretending to compute its
complex component. It does not equate the universal lens with the larger
word-dependent legal set D_h, and does not cover all nonlinear scalar
trajectories.

C59/accepted e9a55f57461f253c remains confined to its ACTUAL H6 with
epsilon1/6. Our m>=128 result is not a transfer of that witness to a
child word whose enclosing parameters changed. No new actual-H6 record
is asserted. Accepted62a7d9329121fd75 remains the finite(2,2,1)
calibration; it is not used as an iteration or family-optimality premise.

### B.3. The original all-split B_n, without changing the denominator

From accepted b98bb807d514e80d, put C_n=1+S_circle(n),
B_n=1+2S_bullet(n), C_0=B_0=1, and

    C_n=max_(k+l+1=n)Omega(C_k,C_l),
    B_n=max_(k+l+1=n)G(C_k,B_l),
    Omega(a,d)=(a+d+sqrt((a+d)^2+4ad))/2,
    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2.

We give the sharper elementary upper estimate needed here, rather than
using an unverified asymptotic of the benchmark. Set p_0=log_2(lambda)>1
and f_0(t)=(1+t)^p_0-1 for t>0. The second derivative of log f_0(t)
with respect to log t is

    p_0 t(1+t)^(p_0-2)
      [(1+t)^p_0-1-p_0 t]/f_0(t)^2>0.

The bracket is positive: it is0 at0 and has positive derivative for
t>0. Convexity at log t and-log t gives
f_0(t)f_0(1/t)>=f_0(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. Consequently

    Omega(a,d)<=(a^(1/p_0)+d^(1/p_0))^p_0.

Indeed the proposed right side X>=max(a,d) obeys(X-a)(X-d)>=2ad,
where equality defines the positive root Omega; zero arguments follow
by continuity. Induction over ALL splits in the ORIGINAL recurrence
therefore gives C_n<=(n+1)^p_0. The inequality G(a,d)<=4a+d follows
by squaring sqrt(d^2+8ad)<=d+4a. A second all-split induction gives

    B_n<=1+4n^p_0.                                     (B27)

For an actual split k+l+1=n it uses (k+1)^p_0+l^p_0<=n^p_0;
this follows by integrating the increasing derivative of x^p_0 and
includes l=0. No reoptimized or selected denominator sequence appears.

The exact original enclosing-m mass in (B21) satisfies

    M_m/lambda^m=(1+1/m)^m
      [a_m/(c_m-2)+(1-a_m/(c_m-2))(2/c_m)^m] ->exp(1).

Here a_m/(c_m-2)->1 and2/c_m is uniformly bounded below1. Also
(2^m-1)^p_0/lambda^m->1. Consequently any restricted family with V<=gamma/(2M_m-1) obeys
limsup B_(2^m-1) sup V<=2gamma/exp(1). This applies with the constants
and precisely stated domains in Sections4 and6 of the present draft.
It does not assert convergence of any actual family supremum.

## Appendix C. Full obtuse-family comparison on ALL remaining original pairs

### C.1. A reserve bound retaining the actual first-prefix ratio

For this appendix only put r_0=A/T and e_0=E/N. These are positive
scalars, not the terminal vector r. Suppose r_0,e_0<=1/2. Define

    C_res(r_0,e_0)=
      r_0 sqrt(e_0)/[sqrt(1+e_0 r_0^2)+r_0 sqrt(e_0)],
            if r_0^2<=e_0/(1+2e_0);
      (r_0^2+e_0)/[2(1+e_0)], otherwise.              (C1)

Then for eta>0,

    2M eta/R^2<=C_res(r_0,e_0).                       (C2)

Here is the complete derivation. If eta<=u alpha, the calculation in
Section5 gives2M eta/R^2<=r_0^2/2. Otherwise set
zeta=eta-u alpha>0, b=zeta/v, x=Mv and B=Mu as in that section.
The retained physical transport gives b<=alpha sqrt(e_0), and

    R^2>=T^2+(x+b)^2,
    2M eta<=2alpha(A-alpha)+2xb.

For a proposed C in(0,1/2), completing the x square bounds
2M eta-C R^2 above by

    2A alpha-[2+2e_0-e_0/C]alpha^2-C T^2.             (C3)

Thus it is enough that
C>=max_(0<=a<=r_0){2r_0 a-D_C a^2},
where a=alpha/T and D_C=2+2e_0-e_0/C. In the first branch of (C1),
C is the positive root of C^2+2e_0 r_0^2 C-e_0 r_0^2=0 and satisfies
C<=e_0/(1+2e_0), hence D_C<=1. The displayed quadratic increases up
to a=r_0, where its value is e_0 r_0^2(1/C-2)=C.
In the second branch D_C>=1 and C D_C=r_0^2; the maximum occurs at
a=r_0/D_C and is r_0^2/D_C=C. These calculations prove (C3)<=0.
Both branch values are<=1/4: in the second branch use r_0^2<=1/4,
e_0<=1/2; in the first evaluate the root polynomial at1/4, obtaining
1/16-e_0 r_0^2/2>=0. Since D_C<=2 when C<=1/2, the same quadratic
maximum is at least its value r_0^2/2 obtained from D_C=2 at a=r_0/2.
Thus C_res>=r_0^2/2, which also covers the first case eta<=u alpha.
This proves (C2), including the common branch boundary.

Because eta<=w alpha and alpha/R<=r_0, define the following explicit
upper bound on the residual after its terminal norm penalty:

    H_res(r_0,e_0;M,N)=
      C_res/M-sigma C_res^2/(4M^2 r_0^2),
                   if sigma C_res<=2M r_0^2;
      r_0^2/sigma, otherwise.                         (C4)

Indeed x=eta/R^2 belongs to[0,C_res/(2M)], and
2eta/R^2-sigma w^2/R^2<=2x-sigma x^2/r_0^2. Maximizing that concave
quadratic on its explicit interval gives (C4). For eta<=0 the residual
is nonpositive and the same nonnegative upper bound remains valid.
Using (15) and the full-norm inequality with no residual lift gives

    V<=E^2 C(T,A,M)/sigma+H_res(r_0,e_0;M,N).           (C5)

All of (C1)--(C5) have been proved here, not inferred from an optimizer.

### C.2. Why a long prefix cannot evade the reserve price

Fix A,M,N,E and put Q=M+N+E. For T=A/r_0 the normalization factor is

    D_ref=2(T+A+Q)-1=d/r_0+b,
    d=2A, b=2(A+Q)-1>d.

For0<r_0,e_0<=1/2, we claim D_ref H_res is nondecreasing in r_0.
This is precisely nonincrease as the physical prefix T gets larger.
Write C=C_res and S=d/r_0+b temporarily. First (S C)'>=0, with the
derivative taken in r_0 at fixed e_0,d,b. On the first branch of (C1),

    S C=sqrt(e_0)(d+b r_0)/
                         [sqrt(1+e_0 r_0^2)+r_0 sqrt(e_0)].

Its derivative has the sign of
b-d[e_0 r_0+sqrt(e_0)sqrt(1+e_0 r_0^2)]>=b-d>=0:
the two terms in brackets are at most1/4 and3/4, respectively.
On the second branch,

    (S C)'=[d(1-e_0/r_0^2)+2b r_0]/[2(1+e_0)].

The branch condition implies e_0/r_0^2<=1+2e_0 and e_0<=r_0.
For the latter, e_0<=r_0^2/(1-2r_0^2)<=r_0 follows from r_0<=1/2.
Therefore the numerator is at least -2d e_0+2b r_0>=0.

On the saturated branch of (C4), S H_res=S r_0^2/sigma increases.
On its other branch put J=S C^2/r_0^2. If J'<=0, the just-proved
(S C)'>=0 proves monotonicity. If J'>0, its branch condition implies
sigma/(4M)<=r_0^2/(2C), and consequently

    M(S H_res)'=(S C)'-[sigma/(4M)]J'
       >=(S C)'-[r_0^2/(2C)]J'
        =C[S'/2+S/r_0]
        =C[d/(2r_0^2)+b/r_0]>0.

Both formulas are continuous at every branch boundary, so the claim
holds throughout. The two possible terms E^2 C(T,A,M)/sigma,
after multiplication by D_ref, also decrease with T: their denominators
are T^2 or(T+A+M)^2, and their numerators have the form2T+constant,
with exactly the positive derivative check given in Section6. Thus the
ENTIRE right side of (C5), normalized by D_ref, decreases with T.

### C.3. An explicit finite bound for every non-nested-second pair

Choose a number rho with

    sqrt2-1<=rho<=1/2, c=2+rho,
    A<=rho min(T,Q), E<=rho min(M,N), Q=M+N+E.         (C6)

Every remaining ORIGINAL pair has (C6) with rho=c_m-2. Indeed the
adjacent H_k on each side of the first selected checkpoint gives
A<=rho T and A<=rho Q. If that checkpoint lies outside the H_l
immediately preceding the second checkpoint, the original middle block
contains the entirety of that H_l, so M>=M_l. Also N>=M_l, and
E<=rho M_l proves the second part of (C6). No chronological query or
enclosing parameter is changed. This exhausts the complement of Section6.

By C.2, replace T by A/rho ONLY in the scalar upper bound. This is not
a claim to construct a new legal trajectory or a new word. At r_0=rho,
the proof of (C3), evaluated at C=rho/2 and e_0<=rho, gives
C_res(rho,e_0)<=rho/2: its upper quadratic is bounded by
2(1-rho)rho^2<=rho/2, using4rho(1-rho)<=1.
Since maximizing a fixed concave quadratic over a larger interval
cannot reduce its maximum, H_res is then at most

    H_bar(M,N;rho)=
      rho/(2M)-sigma/(16M^2), if sigma<=4rho M;
      rho^2/sigma, otherwise.                         (C7)

Define two explicit functions of the ACTUAL remaining masses:

    UB_0=(2Q-1)[H_bar+E^2/(sigma M^2)],
    UB_1=(2cQ-1)[H_bar+rho^2 E^2/(sigma M^2)].          (C8)

Then EVERY legal obtuse tuple on a word with (C6) satisfies

    (2(T+A+Q)-1)V<=max(UB_0,UB_1).                       (C9)

For the pair branch in C(T,A,M), after T=A/rho its non-residual
term is rho^2 E^2/(sigma M^2). Multiplication by the normalization
factor increases with A. Since A<=rho Q, its maximum occurs at
A=rho Q, where T=Q and T+A+Q=cQ. This gives UB_1.

For the other branch put B=T+A+M. After the same scalar substitution,
B ranges from M to M+(1+rho)Q as A ranges from0 to rho Q. Its bound is

    [2(B+N+E)-1][H_bar+E^2/(sigma B^2)].

The expression is convex in B>0: its non-affine part is
(E^2/sigma)[2/B+(2N+2E-1)/B^2], with positive second derivative.
Its maximum on the interval is therefore at an endpoint. At B=M it
is UB_0. At the other endpoint, B>=cM>=M/rho since c rho>=1, and
its normalization factor is2cQ-1; the value is at most UB_1. This proves
(C9). The limiting endpoint A=0 is only part of an upper relaxation;
the actual first checkpoint still has A>0.

### C.4. Uniform analytical asymptotic price of that finite bound

Here m tends to infinity along the ORIGINAL H_m. For all m>=1024,

    rho<=5/12, c<=29/12, c rho^2<3/7.                 (C10)

The original total mass S=M_m tends to infinity. We prove that the
supremum of (2S-1)V over ALL non-nested-second pairs and ALL their
legal parameters has limsup at most13/10. This is a uniform assertion,
not a fixed-pair or fixed-parameter calculation.

Take any sequence of those words, pairs and legal tuples. If Q remains
bounded along a subsequence, then A,E remain bounded by (C6), while
T=S-A-Q tends to infinity. Bound (18) gives

    (2S-1)V
      <=(2S-1)[A^2+E^2 max(A^2/M^2,1)]/(sigma T^2)->0.

This convergence is uniform when Q is bounded, since M,N>=1. It remains
to consider subsequences Q->infinity, for which use the explicit (C9).
Pass to a further subsequence such that t=N/M converges in[0,infinity].
Such a subsequence exists by applying bounded subsequence selection to
t/(1+t). The limit of rho is sqrt2-1; the larger rational bounds (C10)
will suffice throughout.

If t tends to0, then M->infinity, E/M<=rho N/M->0,
sigma/M->0 and Q/M->1. Equation (C7) gives M H_bar->rho/2.
The other term vanishes because
E^2/(sigma M)<=rho^2 N^2/[(2N-1)M]<=rho^2 N/M->0.
Thus limsup UB_1<=c rho<=145/144<13/10 and limsup UB_0<=rho<1.

If t tends to infinity, then N->infinity, E/N<=rho M/N->0,
Q/N->1 and H_bar=rho^2/sigma eventually. Consequently

    limsup UB_1<=c rho^2(1+rho^2)<1,
    limsup UB_0<=2rho^2<1,

using (C10). This includes bounded M.

Suppose now0<t<infinity in the limit. Then M,N both tend to infinity,
so sigma/(2N)->1. For fixed M,N, both UB_0,UB_1 increase with E;
replace E by rho min(M,N) in this scalar upper bound. Define

    q(t)=1+t+rho min(1,t),
    h(t)=rho-t/4 for t<=2rho, and rho^2/t for t>=2rho.

The limiting upper functions are

    ub_0(t)=q(t)[h(t)+rho^2 min(1,t)^2/t],
    ub_1(t)=c q(t)[h(t)+rho^4 min(1,t)^2/t].             (C11)

These expressions are only scalar bounds derived from actual rows;
their entire range is now priced analytically.

For0<t<=2rho<1,

    ub_1(t)=c[1+(1+rho)t][rho-(1/4-rho^4)t]
      <=(29/12)[1+(17/12)t][5/12-(7/32)t]
      <=1574381/1233792<13/10.                        (C12)

The first inequality uses rho^4<1/32 and t<=5/6; both right-side
factors are positive. For the second, the quadratic has constant5/12,
linear coefficient107/288 and negative quadratic coefficient119/384.
Its unrestricted maximum is5/12+(107/288)^2/(119/96)
=54289/102816; multiplying by29/12 gives the stated rational value.
This is a symbolic square completion, not a numerical evaluation.

For2rho<=t<=1, expand

    ub_1(t)=c[rho^2/t+rho^2(1+rho)
                       +rho^4 t+rho^4(1+rho)t^2].

Its second derivative is positive, so its maximum on this interval
is at an endpoint. The first endpoint is covered by (C12). At t=1,

    ub_1(1)=c^2 rho^2(1+rho^2)
       <=3553225/2985984<13/10.                        (C13)

For t>=1, ub_1(t)=c rho^2(1+rho^2)[1+(1+rho)/t]
decreases, so (C13) covers the whole remaining interval.

The other function is smaller than1 throughout. For t<=2rho,

    ub_0(t)<=[1+2rho(1+rho)]rho<=785/864<1,

because1/4-rho^2>=0. For2rho<=t<=1 it is
rho^2[1/t+(1+rho)+t+(1+rho)t^2], a convex function. Its endpoint
values are covered by the preceding bound and
ub_0(1)=2c rho^2<=6/7. For t>=1 it decreases from that last value.
This completes the analytical bound for every limit of t.

The sequence argument proves uniformity. If a limsup of the supremum
exceeded13/10, choose a violating pair and tuple at each of a sequence
of increasing m, with a fixed positive excess. Its Q values have either
a bounded subsequence or a subsequence tending to infinity. The former
case tends to0. In the latter, a further subsequence of N/M falls into
one of the three cases just proved, each bounded by13/10. This is a
contradiction. Exact attainment of a parameter supremum is not needed.

### C.5. Whole declared family, original denominator, and exact scope

Section6 bounds the entire nested-before-second pair class, for m>=1024,
by gamma_nested K with gamma_nested=133613/134064<1. Appendix C covers
every complementary pair and gives limsup V/K<=13/10, because
K=1/(2M_m-1). Thus the supremum over ALL pairs and ALL parameters of
(1)--(6) obeys

    limsup V_m^obt/K_(2^m-1)(H_m)<=13/10.              (C14)

Appendix B proves COMPLETE F_ray=K on these same words; the full
Huber-augmented universal-lens and full word-dependent complex channels
are at least K. Hence for every epsilon>0, all sufficiently large m
satisfy V_m^obt<=(13/10+epsilon) times EACH of those complete references.
No maximum over a sampled checkpoint subset has been substituted.
No equality with the complex component or comparison with all nonlinear
scalar functions is asserted.

Finally Appendix B proves, for the ORIGINAL all-split denominator,

    limsup B_(2^m-1)/(2M_m-1)<=2/exp(1).

Multiplying the two nonnegative bounded asymptotic factors gives (0):

    limsup B_(2^m-1) V_m^obt<=13/[5 exp(1)]<1.         (C15)

The last inequality follows from exp(1)>65/24>13/5. This is the
requested whole-declared-family obstruction: every parameter and every
original checkpoint pair is covered, with exact finite dependence (C9)
on the word conditions (C6). The separate structural class is covered
by (26). The original target is not established, and no claim to exclude
obtuse endpoints in a different family is made. A next mechanism must
change a named hypothesis from Section8; retuning this complete family
on these original words cannot reach the original benchmark strength.
